# Mukhtasar al-Athar: The Summary of the Narrations

<!-- edition-intro:begin -->
## Introduction

This is a manual of religious practice, laid out topic by topic the way a working handbook of law and worship is built. Its nine chapters move in the settled order such books follow: they open with the duty to seek knowledge, then turn to purity and the washing that readies the body, to the standing of formal prayer, to supplication in its many forms, to the rites owed the dead, to the alms due from wealth, to the fast of Ramadan, to the pilgrimage to the House, and finally to the wider business of the community — warfare, governance, and the doing of justice. Read straight through, it is a compact tour of the obligations a believer is expected to carry, from the most private act of cleansing to the ordering of a state.

The title carries a numeral, which places this volume as the first of a set rather than a whole standing alone; what follows here is its own opening, translated and printed directly below.

A word about who is speaking. This is a transmitted report: the text relays received teaching rather than voicing a single named author's opinions. When you meet a first person on the page, it belongs to the narrator carrying the material forward, not to an "I" who is confiding in you — read it as testimony being passed along a chain, not as memoir. Mistaking the one for the other will make the book sound more personal than it is.

Much of the working vocabulary stays in Arabic because the concepts have no clean English equivalent. The names of the obligations themselves — zakat, the pilgrimage, the fast, jihad — recur as fixed technical terms, each pointing to a defined act with its own conditions rather than to a loose English gloss. Treat these words as anchors: when one returns, it means the same precise thing it meant before, and the chapters are in large part an unfolding of what each one requires.
<!-- edition-intro:end -->

## 1. The First Obligation: Seeking Knowledge

In the name of God, the Most Merciful, the Most Compassionate.

God, mighty and majestic, said: ﴿يَرْفَعِ اللَّهُ الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا مِنكُمْ وَالَّذِينَ أُوتُوا الْعِلْمَ دَرَجَاتٍ﴾, "Allah will raise those of you who believe and those who have been given knowledge by degrees" [Surah al-Mujadalah: 11]. And the Almighty said: ﴿هَلْ يَسْتَوِي الَّذِينَ يَعْلَمُونَ وَالَّذِينَ لَا يَعْلَمُونَ إِنَّمَا يَتَذَكَّرُ أُولُو الْأَلْبَابِ﴾, "Are those who know equal to those who do not know? Only those possessed of understanding take heed" [Surah al-Zumar: 9]. And He said: ﴿بَلْ هُوَ آيَاتٌ بَيِّنَاتٌ فِي صُدُورِ الَّذِينَ أُوتُوا الْعِلْمَ﴾, "Rather, it is clear signs preserved in the breasts of those who have been given knowledge" [Surah al-Ankabut: 49].

The Messenger of God (صلى الله عليه وآله وسلم) said: "Whomever God intends good for, He grants understanding of religion." And he said: "Four things are obligatory upon every person of reason and intellect among my community: to listen to knowledge, to preserve it, to act upon it, and to spread it." And he said: "Learn from the scholar of my household, for whoever learns from the scholar of my household will be saved from the Fire." And he said: "There is no rest in life save for one who speaks with knowledge or one who listens with understanding." And he said: "This knowledge is borne in every generation by its upright ones, who repel from it the distortions of the ignorant, the false interpretations of those who go to excess, and the misappropriations of the fabricators." And he said: "When a man goes out in search of knowledge, God records good deeds for his every footstep; and when he meets the scholar and the two recall together something of the command of God, the angels spread their wings over them, and they are called from above: God has forgiven you both." And he said: "The first stage of knowledge is silence; the second is listening; the third is spreading it; and the fourth is acting upon it."

Ali (عليه السلام) said: "Seeking knowledge is an obligation upon every Muslim, man and woman." And he said: "Whoever buys and sells without gaining understanding of religion has plunged into usury, then plunged into it again." And he said: "Four things, were riding-beasts to be driven hard toward them until they were worn out, that would be a small price: that the servant place his hope in none but his Lord, that he fear nothing but his own sin, that the ignorant not be ashamed to learn, and that the scholar, when asked about what he does not know, say, I do not know."

It is reported from Jafar ibn Muhammad (ع) that he said: "Seek knowledge, and adorn it with forbearance and dignity. Be humble toward those whom you teach, and do not be tyrannical scholars, lest your falsehood undo your truth." And he said: "Were a young man of our Shia brought to me who had not gained understanding in religion, I would discipline him well." And he said: "The believer does not cease to bequeath to his household knowledge and righteous conduct until he brings them all into Paradise, so that not one of them is lost, neither young nor old, neither servant nor neighbor. And the disobedient servant does not cease to bequeath to his household ignorance and evil conduct until he brings them all into the Fire, so that not one of them is lost, neither young nor old, neither servant nor neighbor."

## 2. Purity: Water, Ablution, and the Body Made Ready

The comprehensive chapters on purity begin here.

**On the merits of purity**

God Almighty said: *"Indeed, God loves those who constantly repent and loves those who purify themselves"* [Surah al-Baqarah: 222]. And He said: *"Within it are men who love to purify themselves, and God loves those who purify themselves"* [Surah al-Tawbah: 108]. And He said: *"And He sent down upon you rain from the sky to purify you thereby, and to remove the harm of Satan from you, and to fortify your hearts and make your feet firm thereby"* [Surah al-Anfal: 11].

> خُذْ مِنْ أَمْوَلِهِمْ صَدَقَةًۭ تُطَهِّرُهُمْ وَتُزَكِّيهِم بِهَا

The Messenger of God (ص) said: "God does not accept prayer except with purity." And he said: "God will gather my community on the Day of Resurrection among the nations, radiant and distinguished by the traces of ablution." And he said: "When I was taken up to the heavens, I was asked concerning what the denizens of the highest assembly were disputing. I said, I do not know, and then it was made known to me: the thorough completion of ablution in times of cold, the walking of feet to the congregations, and the awaiting of prayer after prayer."

From Ali (ع) it is reported that he said: "Ablution is half of faith." And he said: "Whoever purifies himself and performs the purification well, then walks to the mosque, is in a state of prayer so long as nothing occurs to break it." He (ع) would perform ablution for every prayer even when nothing had occurred to break it, seeking thereby the merit of purity and its reward, for the excellence that has been reported concerning it.

**On the purity and impurity of water**

> وَأَنزَلْنَا مِنَ ٱلسَّمَآءِ مَآءًۭ طَهُورًۭا

God Almighty said: *"And We sent down from the sky pure water"* [Surah al-Furqan: 48]. And He said: *"And He sent down upon you from the sky water to purify you thereby"* [Surah al-Anfal: 11]. And He also said: *"Have you not seen that God sent down water from the sky, then caused it to flow as springs in the earth?"* [Surah al-Zumar: 21]. By this He, exalted be His praise, indicated that the water of the earth originates from the sky, and the reports have confirmed its purity.

> أُحِلَّ لَكُمْ صَيْدُ ٱلْبَحْرِ وَطَعَامُهُۥ مَتَعًۭا لَّكُمْ وَلِلسَّيَّارَةِ

The Messenger of God (ص) said: "Water is a purifier; nothing renders it impure." And he said concerning the sea: "Its water is a purifier and its dead are lawful." By this he meant what is extracted from it of that which is permissible to eat, which then dies after being brought out, not what dies while still within it. For God Almighty said: *"Lawful to you is the game of the sea and its food, as provision for you and for travelers"* [Surah al-Maidah: 96], that is, what is drawn from it and eaten outside, not what is eaten beneath the water or at its floor. By "its dead" he meant what dies after having been brought out alive. As for what dies within the water before it is caught, it is not lawful to eat, according to the position of the Ahl al-Bayt (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), and that is the fish which floats. We will address this in the chapter on foods, God willing.

As for water that occurs naturally, in whose making man has no hand, it is pure. God Almighty made clear in His Book, and His Messenger (ص) made clear in his speech, that if something is cast into water and alters it, transforming it from the attribute of water, the ruling of water is removed from it, and it is ascribed instead to whatever has been cast into it and prevailed over it. This is like water into which dates, raisins, or honey are thrown: if they do not change it and remain undiscernible within it, its ruling remains the ruling of water, until what was cast into it becomes evident and manifest, altering its color or its taste. At that point it becomes a fermented drink, the name of water ceases to apply to it, and purification with it is no longer permitted.

> وَإِن كُنتُم مَّرْضَىٰٓ أَوْ عَلَىٰ سَفَرٍ أَوْ جَآءَ أَحَدٌۭ مِّنكُم مِّنَ ٱلْغَآئِطِ أَوْ لَمَسْتُمُ ٱلنِّسَآءَ فَلَمْ تَجِدُوا۟ مَآءًۭ فَتَيَمَّمُوا۟ صَعِيدًۭا طَيِّبًۭا فَٱمْسَحُوا۟ بِوُجُوهِكُمْ وَأَيْدِيكُمْ

I know of no disagreement among the Muslims on this matter, except for the position held alone by Abu Hanifah and those of his followers who agreed with him. He said that it is permissible to perform ablution with date wine in particular, though not with the other cooked beverages. His companion Abu Yusuf disagreed with him in this and said that ablution is valid with nothing but water. Muhammad ibn al-Hasan said: "If you do not find water, perform ablution with date wine, then perform tayammum." This is a position without meaning. For God Almighty said: *"And if you find no water, then betake yourselves to clean earth"* [Surah al-Nisa: 43]. If date wine is not water, there is no sense in using it for purification; and if it is water, there is no sense in the tayammum. What led Abu Hanifah into this was the report that reached him, that the Messenger of God (ص) performed ablution with date wine on the Night of the Jinn. This hadith is not established; and even were it established, that drink would not be what people know today as date wine. Rather, the Prophet (ص) used to steep dates in the waterskin from which he drank, in order to sweeten the water for him when it was brackish. He would drink from it and perform ablution with it so long as it had not changed. We have already mentioned that there is no harm in this, and that it does not strip the ruling of water from the water unless it alters it and transforms it from its attribute.

The same holds for milk that mixes with water: if the milk is not discernible within it, it remains water in its state until the milk prevails, at which point its ruling becomes the ruling of milk and the name of water is removed from it. Likewise for whatever drinks and other things mix with water. Upon this same principle stands whatever impurity mixes with water and enters into it: if it becomes apparent within it and alters something of its color, its taste, or its smell, the water is impure and takes on the ruling of that impurity. But if it does not appear within it and cannot be discerned, the water remains in its state, lawful to drink and to use for purification. This is the agreed position of the Ahl al-Bayt, established from them, and those who claim mastery of reasoned inquiry among the companions of al-Shafii, the people of hadith, and many attributed to jurisprudence among the general body have concurred with them upon it.

If the color, smell, or taste of water changes through something other than impurity that has mixed into it, as with stagnant water that sits long in its place and is thereby altered, it remains upon its purity, for it has not been changed by impurity. Ali said: "Nothing renders water impure," meaning so long as the ruling of water applies to it, as we have described. He was asked about running water that passes by a carcass, excrement, and blood, whether one may perform ablution from it and drink from it, and he said: "Yes," meaning so long as none of those things is discernible within it. He was asked about a pool in which beasts had urinated and dropped dung and in which the one in janabah had bathed, and he said: "There is no harm in it. The Messenger of God (ص) once halted with his companions on a journey at a pool, and their mounts would urinate in it and drop dung in it, and they would bathe in it, perform ablution from it, and drink from it."

> وَإِن كُنتُم مَّرْضَىٰٓ أَوْ عَلَىٰ سَفَرٍ أَوْ جَآءَ أَحَدٌۭ مِّنكُم مِّنَ ٱلْغَآئِطِ أَوْ لَمَسْتُمُ ٱلنِّسَآءَ فَلَمْ تَجِدُوا۟ مَآءًۭ فَتَيَمَّمُوا۟ صَعِيدًۭا طَيِّبًۭا فَٱمْسَحُوا۟ بِوُجُوهِكُمْ وَأَيْدِيكُمْ

He was asked about a small ablution basin near the mosque, into which a menstruating woman puts her hand and a boy puts his hand, and he said: "Perform ablution from it, for the water is defiled by nothing." He was asked about a pool beside a village, into which a child and the beasts urinate and drop dung, and he said: "If something troubles your heart concerning it, do this," and he gestured with his hand, meaning stir the water, "then drink and perform ablution, for the religion is not a narrow thing." And God Almighty said: *"And He has laid upon you no hardship in religion"* [Surah al-Hajj: 78]. He was asked about a pool in which there was a carcass, and he said: "If the water is dominant and no smell is found in it, then perform ablution from it." And he said: "If the water is two cubits by two cubits at a depth of two cubits, nothing renders it impure except what prevails over it and changes its color, its smell, or its taste," meaning from impurities. The reports of this kind narrated from them are many. Such likewise is the ruling of a well in which an animal dies: if its water has been altered, it is drawn out until the alteration is gone and the water prevails again; and if nothing of it has been altered, there is no harm. Whatever died in it is taken out and cast away lest it alter the water. There is no sound ruling regarding water except this.

If those who oppose this were held to their own analogy in the matter, no purification would ever be valid for them upon their own principle. For when one who cleanses himself touches his private part with the water, the water mixes with the impurity there by the very act of touching, and by their words and analogy that water would become impure. Likewise the further water that flows toward it would, by contact with that moisture, become defiled, so that upon their analogy it could never purify. Such too is the garment washed in a wash-tub that holds impurity: everything poured over it, mingling with the moisture of that water, could never be purified upon their analogy. And such too is when a thing possessing blood dies in a well: they say a set number of buckets must be drawn out, yet they also hold that the water of the well, before that number is drawn out, is impure, and that if any of it fell into another well, that well too would be defiled. When they have drawn out the stated number, one bucket short, the well is by their reckoning still not pure; and when that last bucket is drawn out, it becomes pure by their reckoning, while the water within that bucket is impure by their reckoning. Yet as it rises, much of it spatters back into the well and into the bucket, and that bucket had been plunged into the well water they claimed became pure at the final draw, and it carries the moisture of impure water. So by their own analogy it has spoiled it, and upon their analogy it could never be purified. The proofs in this are many, and our condition of brevity prevents us from exhausting them.

**On what is obligatory in ablution and what is obligatory in the greater washing**

God Almighty said: *"O you who believe, when you rise to pray, wash your faces and your hands to the elbows, and wipe your heads and your feet to the ankles. And if you are in a state of janabah, then purify yourselves. But if you are ill, or on a journey, or one of you has come from the place of relieving himself, or you have touched women and find no water, then betake yourselves to clean earth and wipe your faces and hands with it"* [Surah al-Maidah: 6].

Jafar ibn Muhammad (ع) said: His words "when you rise to pray" mean from sleep; and His words "or one of you has come from the place of relieving himself" are an indirect expression for the occurrence of ritual impurity, the ghait being the low-lying ground, the place to which people would withdraw for the need of a person; and His words "or you have touched women" mean intercourse, expressed indirectly by touching, for touching is contact. When a person has purified himself, his purity is not broken except by sleep that overcomes him and comes between him and his awareness of what may have issued from him. As for one who dozes lightly while still perceiving what happens to him and does not sink into heavy sleep, his ablution is not broken. Ablution is broken by urine, by excrement, and by wind issuing from the posterior, and by everything that issues from the posterior or the front, whether blood, discharge, pus, worms, moisture, or the like, all of which break ablution. What issues from other than these two outlets, whether blood, vomit, discharge, or anything else, does not break it. Ablution is not broken by touching with the hand, by contact short of intercourse, by a kiss, by direct touching, by touching the male organ or the private part, nor by food or drink. Yet one washes from the body whatever impurity has reached it, and it is recommended to cleanse oneself of grease and other things that carry an odor, without this being obligatory in a binding sense such that prayer is not valid without it. Rather cleanliness and purity are recommended. Whoever is certain that he is in purity and then doubts whether he has broken it, without being certain of the breaking, owes no fresh purification. But whoever has broken his purity and then doubts whether he has purified himself must purify himself. All of this is the position of the Ahl al-Bayt. With them, nothing breaks ablution except what issues from the two outlets, the front and the posterior.

As for al-madhi, the thin fluid that issues from the male organ through the desire for intercourse, it requires only ablution, not the greater washing. And al-wadhi, the thin fluid that follows urine, likewise requires only ablution and not the greater washing. If a man has intercourse, or otherwise enters janabah, and performs the greater washing before urinating, and then after the washing water issues from his urethra, it is a remnant of the janabah, and he must repeat the washing. But if he had urinated before washing, the urine has expelled the remnant of the janabah; and if anything issues after the urination or the washing, only ablution is required, and no washing is upon him. For this reason it is fitting that whoever has had intercourse or a nocturnal emission should not perform the greater washing until he urinates. Whoever has a nocturnal emission and finds the gushing fluid must perform the greater washing. If he dreams that he is having relations but finds no wetness, nothing is required of him; and if he supposes that he emitted in sleep, the greater washing becomes obligatory upon him only by the actual emergence of semen. Whoever has relations short of penetration while awake and does not emit owes no washing until he emits. But whoever penetrates the private part owes the washing, whether or not he emits, once the two circumcised parts have met, that is, once the glans, which is the head of the male organ, has disappeared within the private part. When that has occurred, the washing is obligatory, whether or not he emits. All of this is the position of the Ahl al-Bayt.

**On the manner of ablution and the greater washing and their proper etiquette**

It has been transmitted to us from the Ahl al-Bayt that they commanded the covering of the private parts when going to relieve oneself, the seeking of a place for urine and excrement, and the taking of whatever concealment can be found for it, and withdrawing as far from people as one is able. They forbade urinating and defecating in stagnant water, on the brinks of rivers and wells, on the roads, beneath fruit-bearing trees, among the graves, and wherever it would bring harm to people. One who intends this should not face the qiblah nor turn his back to it, and should guard against anything of it reaching himself or his garment. They commanded whoever enters the place of relieving himself, or intends to relieve himself, to invoke the name of God and to seek refuge from the accursed Satan; and when he has finished, to praise God and give thanks for what He has made easy for him of the wholesomeness of his food and drink and the removal of their harm from him. They permitted urinating and defecating into a basin and into vessels, and they commanded the drawing out of the male organ from urine, so that what remains in the shaft may come forth. If he finds water, he washes it; and if he finds no water, he wipes his urethra upon clean ground, a stone, a cloth, or the like. If he has defecated, he wipes with stones, clods of earth, rags, or the like, wiping repeatedly until he is clean, and looking at what he wiped with last to see whether anything still clings to it. They forbade wiping with any food, with bone, with dried dung, and with fruit stones, by which they meant the pits of fruits, that is, their kernels, and they forbade cleansing with charcoal.

> يَٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ آمَنُوٓا۟ إِذَا قُمْتُمْ إِلَى ٱلصَّلَوٰةِ فَٱغْسِلُوا۟ وُجُوهَكُمْ وَأَيْدِيَكُمْ إِلَى ٱلْمَرَافِقِ وَٱمْسَحُوا۟ بِرُئُوسِكُمْ وَأَرْجُلَكُمْ إِلَى ٱلْكَعْبَيْنِ ۚ وَإِن كُنتُمْ جُنُبًۭا فَٱطَّهَّرُوا۟ ۚ وَإِن كُنتُم مَّرْضَىٰٓ أَوْ عَلَىٰ سَفَرٍ أَوْ جَآءَ أَحَدٌۭ مِّنكُم مِّنَ ٱلْغَآئِطِ أَوْ لَمَسْتُمُ ٱلنِّسَآءَ فَلَمْ تَجِدُوا۟ مَآءًۭ فَتَيَمَّمُوا۟ صَعِيدًۭا طَيِّبًۭا فَٱمْسَحُوا۟ بِوُجُوهِكُمْ وَأَيْدِيكُم مِّنْهُ

They commanded whoever intends ablution or the greater washing to make the intention of it. If he does not intend it, and washes only to cool himself, or performs ablution in that manner, or does so to instruct another and does not intend it for himself, it does not suffice him, and he must repeat whatever was made obligatory upon him with an intention he forms within himself. They recommended that whoever intends this invoke the name of God at its beginning. Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali (ع) said: "Whoever names God over his ablution, his whole body is purified," meaning of sins, God willing. As for its purifying him of janabah before he performs the greater washing, no; but he is encouraged toward it. And whoever does not name God has not thereby spoiled his ablution. They commanded the washing of the two hands before entering them into the vessel; but if he enters them into the vessel while they are clean, nothing is upon him, though washing is better. Then he cleanses himself from excrement and urine and from everything that issues from their two outlets. There is no obligatory cleansing for wind that issues from the posterior, though it is recommended, and some of them used to do it, seeking its merit. He begins the cleansing with the front, then lowers his hand to the posterior and clears away whatever moisture or smear is there until it is gone, pouring the water with his right hand and washing with his left, then rubbing with water after the cleansing until the smell is gone. He does not cleanse with his right hand except from some excuse. Then he rinses his mouth and draws water into his nose three times, and this is of the Sunnah that ought not to be left off; yet if one leaves it off without intending to abandon it, nothing is upon him. Then he washes his face from its top to its bottom. The man gathers his beard in his hand and wipes over it, and if he runs water through it, that is good. He washes it three times, and if he washes it twice, or once completely, that suffices him, though three is better; there is no merit beyond three. Such too is the washing of the hands. Then he washes his arm to the elbows, doing this three times.

In the washing of the hands, as in the washing of the face, one time is sufficient and three is better; yet beyond three there is no further merit of obligation. If a man has a ring upon his hand, he must move it so that the water reaches what lies beneath it, and he should leave no place upon his face or his arms that the water has not touched. The face extends from the hairline of the forehead down to the bottom of the chin, and across from the tip of one set of sideburns to the other, taking in the lobes of the ears. He then wipes his head, facing forward and then turning back: he begins from the top and wipes toward the front of his head, then returns from where he began, wiping with the moisture remaining on his hands as far as the hairline at the nape. This suffices done once, and if he wipes three times that is better, though not obligatory. Together with this he wipes his ears, their outer and inner surfaces alike, and if he takes fresh water for a second wiping of them, that is better. Then he wipes the tops of his feet in a wiping motion. This is the meaning of the word of Allah Almighty:

> يَٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ آمَنُوٓا۟ إِذَا قُمْتُمْ إِلَى ٱلصَّلَوٰةِ فَٱغْسِلُوا۟ وُجُوهَكُمْ وَأَيْدِيَكُمْ إِلَى ٱلْمَرَافِقِ وَٱمْسَحُوا۟ بِرُئُوسِكُمْ وَأَرْجُلَكُمْ إِلَى ٱلْكَعْبَيْنِ ۚ وَإِن كُنتُمْ جُنُبًۭا فَٱطَّهَّرُوا۟ ۚ وَإِن كُنتُم مَّرْضَىٰٓ أَوْ عَلَىٰ سَفَرٍ أَوْ جَآءَ أَحَدٌۭ مِّنكُم مِّنَ ٱلْغَآئِطِ أَوْ لَمَسْتُمُ ٱلنِّسَآءَ فَلَمْ تَجِدُوا۟ مَآءًۭ فَتَيَمَّمُوا۟ صَعِيدًۭا طَيِّبًۭا فَٱمْسَحُوا۟ بِوُجُوهِكُمْ وَأَيْدِيكُم مِّنْهُ

"فَاغْسِلُوا وُجُوهَكُمْ وَأَيْدِيَكُمْ إِلَى الْمَرَافِقِ وَامْسَحُوا بِرُءُوسِكُمْ وَأَرْجُلَكُمْ إِلَى الْكَعْبَيْنِ" — "Wash your faces and your hands to the elbows, and wipe your heads and your feet to the heels." [al-Maidah 6:6]

For the Almighty has made obligatory the washing of the face and the hands and the wiping of the head and the feet. If a man washes his feet as well as wiping over them, that is good; and if he passes his fingers between his toes, that too is good and carries reward. He must not put one limb before another in the ablution, but begins with what Allah began: the face, then the hands, then the head, then the feet. If he begins out of this order and washes an earlier limb after a later one, he returns and washes the later limb again so that the sequence is restored, first things first. If in the midst of his ablution he interrupts it for some matter that did not lead him astray, and then afterward completes what remained of it without beginning the ablution afresh, that suffices, and he need not treat the interruption as spoiling it. This is permitted only if he broke it off for a genuine excuse, not if he did so deliberately. As for the quantity of water used in ablution, there is no fixed measure. People differ in this: some are gentle and content with a little, while others are wasteful and are not satisfied with what suffices another. And in the full and generous performance of ablution there is merit.

The limbs of ablution must be thoroughly wetted with the water as commanded; otherwise the water merely passes over them without effect. Where a hand or a foot has been amputated, the water is passed over the place of amputation. It is recommended to perform ablution before the time of the prayer enters, so that the time may arrive while a person is already in a state of purity and he may pray at its outset. The scholars disapproved of wastefulness with water in ablution and called it a whispering of the devils, yet they permitted a person to dry himself with a garment from the wetness of ablution or of ghusl once he has finished. They commended supplication and the remembrance of Allah while washing each of the limbs of ablution, and after finishing it. They forbade wiping over socks and were severe in this, holding no prayer valid for one who wipes over his socks after a state of ritual impurity, nor for one who follows him in prayer, because that contradicts the Book of Allah, whose mention is exalted. Allah has commanded only the wiping of the feet. Whoever has in his feet an ailment that would be harmed by washing may wipe water over them; and if they are within socks, or bound with dressings, or if some other limb of ablution is so bound, or a splint is set over a wound or a break, the wiping suffices over all of that. Wiping over sandals suffices when the water reaches the tops of the feet, but it does not suffice over anything else, such as a head-cloth or a turban or the like, where there is no excuse of ailment.

Whoever intends the greater washing from janabah or from menstruation begins by performing ablution as we have described, then pours the water over his head and over the whole of his body until the hair is wetted and whatever soiling was on the body is cleansed. He passes his hands over the rest of his body so that no place remains that the water has not reached, passing his hands over all that he can reach. When he has done this he is purified. If any place on his body remains that the water has not touched, he must bring water to it. There is no fixed measure for the water of ghusl, as we said concerning ablution. Ghusl is valid with rainwater at the time of rain, and by the bather's immersing himself in water in a single plunge, provided he passes his hands over his body as described and the water reaches all of it. Ghusl becomes obligatory from janabah, from menstruation, when a disbeliever accepts Islam, and when one who has been unconscious for days, or for a stretch of time he cannot account for, comes to himself, since he cannot be sure whether a state of janabah befell him; and this holds when the unconsciousness was of that severity. Where the unconsciousness was slighter and its circumstances could be perceived and known, so that a person would sense whether a discharge had occurred, then no ghusl is required for it.

> وَإِن كُنتُم مَّرْضَىٰٓ أَوْ عَلَىٰ سَفَرٍ أَوْ جَآءَ أَحَدٌۭ مِّنكُم مِّنَ ٱلْغَآئِطِ أَوْ لَمَسْتُمُ ٱلنِّسَآءَ فَلَمْ تَجِدُوا۟ مَآءًۭ فَتَيَمَّمُوا۟ صَعِيدًۭا طَيِّبًۭا فَٱمْسَحُوا۟ بِوُجُوهِكُمْ وَأَيْدِيكُمْ

The dead must be washed when they die. When the bather performs ghusl from janabah and passes his hands over the limbs of ablution, intending by it both the ghusl and the ablution, that suffices him for both. If a woman experiences intercourse in her sleep and a discharge occurs, ghusl becomes obligatory upon her just as it is upon a man who experiences the like and finds that a discharge has occurred. One upon whom ghusl is due should perform it whenever he is able and not delay it, unless he intends to return to intercourse soon, in which case there is no harm in his delaying the ghusl until he has done so and then washing; and if he washes between the acts, that is better. If he has intercourse again, he washes again. But if the time of a prayer arrives, he should not delay the ghusl, whether or not he intends to return to intercourse. It is disliked for the one in janabah to eat or drink before he has washed his body, and disliked that he trim his nails or shave his hair or be cupped while in that state, and he should not sit in the mosque while in janabah. There is no harm in his passing through it for some need that arises, without sitting, as in the word of Allah Almighty: "وَلَا جُنُبًا إِلَّا عَابِرِي سَبِيلٍ" — "nor while in janabah, except those passing through." [al-Nisa 4:43] If a woman has had intercourse, or become junub, and then menstruates before she has washed, one ghusl suffices her for both once she sees her purity. When a woman washes, she need not undo the braids of her hair, provided she wets it with a wetting that she knows has reached all of it and penetrated to the skin of her head; there is no harm in her washing while it is braided, so long as the braid is not so tight that the wetness cannot enter, and where there is some slackness in it that admits the moisture. If there is a binding upon it, she moves it so that the water reaches beneath. The bather inserts a finger into his navel and passes water within it, and after finishing the ghusl he supplicates with whatever has been apportioned to him and asks Allah to purify him of sins.

**The purity of garments and of the ground**

It is related from the People of the House (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) that every animal whose flesh is lawful to eat has pure skin, feathers, hair, wool, fur, bone, and sinew, provided it was slaughtered in the prescribed manner; but if it died without proper slaughter, all of that from it is impure. The scholars permitted wearing and making use of such a hide as one wears and makes use of a garment that has been touched by impurity, only he does not pray in it. As for an animal whose flesh is not lawful to eat, all of that from it is impure; it too may be put to use, but it is not lawful to pray in it. We shall mention in the chapter on foods what of this is lawful and what is forbidden to eat. Whatever is bought from the markets of the Muslims, when one does not know it to be from a carcass, is treated as pure until that is known, just as it is purchased; and meat sold in the markets of the Muslims is eaten even if the buyer has not ascertained how it was slaughtered, for in such matters the Muslims are to be trusted concerning it.

If a pure garment is struck by urine, semen, prostatic fluid, wine, an intoxicant, blood, pus, the blood of a wound, ordure, or the dung of an animal whose flesh or urine is not lawful, or if that reaches the body while it is wet, one does not pray in it until it is washed. There is no harm in wearing the garment and making use of it before that is washed out of it, only he does not pray in it until it is washed. As for what is slight, such as the blood of fleas and ticks, and what splashes up of water at the time of ablution or ghusl from ground that impurity has reached, or a fly that alights upon impurity and then alights upon the garments, and the like of these small and unnoticed matters, there is no harm in them so long as they do not become manifest to a gross degree; when it becomes gross, it is washed. Whoever has a wound or sores that flow, and cannot hold back their flowing so as to wash it from his body and garment, should guard against it as much as he is able; and what overcomes him of it at the time of prayer does not harm him nor spoil his prayer, and he prays with it, for Allah is most excusing of the servant when He afflicts him. As for the garments of the idolaters, one should not pray in them until they are washed, and one prays in the garments of Muslims unless he knows that impurity is upon them.

If he does not know that there is impurity in it, there is no harm in praying in it, and it is treated as pure until impurity becomes evident in it. Whoever prays in a garment supposing it pure and then, after praying, learns that there had been impurity in it, his prayer stands and nothing is required of him. If he learns of it while he is in the prayer, he removes the garment; and if beneath it is a pure garment that covers him, he builds upon his prayer, but if it was worn alone, he removes it, puts on a pure garment, and prays in it. There is no harm in the sweat of one in janabah or of a menstruating woman, nor in their leavings, when they are Muslims, for the Muslim is pure. Likewise, if a wet garment clings to such a body, that does not render it impure, unless there is upon the body a smear of impurity that clings to the garment, in which case that place of it is washed. And likewise, if there is impurity in a garment and the one wearing it sweats in it, or his body touches, while wet, the place of that impurity, so that its moisture clings to the body, then that is washed. But what reaches the garment or the body of a carcass or of impurity while dry and not moist, from which nothing has clung to it, does not harm it. The urine of every animal whose flesh is lawful to eat is pure with respect to ablution, except one that has been eating impurity or is in the habit of eating it, such as chickens and their like, for then what its droppings touch is washed if it feeds on filth; but if it is confined to pure fodder, there is no harm in its droppings. The mud of rain, so long as it has not been altered by impurity, is pure; and if it is altered by something other than impurity mingling with it, it is likewise pure; but if impurity mingles with it and alters it, then it is impure. If one walking treads upon damp impurity with his shoe and then wipes it upon the ground so that the moisture is gone, it is pure; and if it does not go, it is washed. Likewise, if he walks upon it with his foot and then walks upon pure ground so that the moisture is gone, no washing is required of him; but if it remains, he washes his feet. If impurity reaches the ground and then the sun takes it so that its moisture and its odor are gone from the ground, it has become pure and one may pray upon it. One does not pray upon impure ground, nor in a graveyard, but one may pray in the folds of sheep and the resting-places of camels when they do not know of any impurity present.

**The tooth-stick**

The Messenger of Allah (ص) said: "The tooth-stick is a purifier for the mouth and a means of the Lord's pleasure. Jibril did not come to me but that he charged me with the tooth-stick, until I feared it would be made obligatory upon the front of my mouth." And Ali said: "Three things were given to the Prophets: the tooth-stick, perfume, and wives." And Jafar ibn Muhammad ibn Ali said: "The tooth-stick is half of ablution, and ablution is half of faith, and the tooth-stick drives away phlegm." The Messenger of Allah (ص) said: "Were it not that I would burden my community, I would have made the tooth-stick obligatory with every ablution." Whoever is able for it should not neglect it. And he said: "Use the tooth-stick across, and do not use it lengthwise." He said that rubbing with the thumb and the forefinger at the time of ablution, that is, using them in place of the tooth-stick, suffices in its stead. And he forbade using a tooth-stick of reed, or of basil, or of pomegranate, and said that this stirs up the vein of leprosy.

**The dry ablution**

> يَٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ آمَنُوٓا۟ إِذَا قُمْتُمْ إِلَى ٱلصَّلَوٰةِ فَٱغْسِلُوا۟ وُجُوهَكُمْ وَأَيْدِيَكُمْ إِلَى ٱلْمَرَافِقِ وَٱمْسَحُوا۟ بِرُئُوسِكُمْ وَأَرْجُلَكُمْ إِلَى ٱلْكَعْبَيْنِ ۚ وَإِن كُنتُمْ جُنُبًۭا فَٱطَّهَّرُوا۟ ۚ وَإِن كُنتُم مَّرْضَىٰٓ أَوْ عَلَىٰ سَفَرٍ أَوْ جَآءَ أَحَدٌۭ مِّنكُم مِّنَ ٱلْغَآئِطِ أَوْ لَمَسْتُمُ ٱلنِّسَآءَ فَلَمْ تَجِدُوا۟ مَآءًۭ فَتَيَمَّمُوا۟ صَعِيدًۭا طَيِّبًۭا فَٱمْسَحُوا۟ بِوُجُوهِكُمْ وَأَيْدِيكُم مِّنْهُ

Allah Almighty said: "يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا إِذَا قُمْتُمْ إِلَى الصَّلَاةِ فَاغْسِلُوا وُجُوهَكُمْ" — "O you who believe, when you rise to pray, wash your faces," [al-Maidah 6:6] down to His word: "فَإِنْ لَمْ تَجِدُوا مَاءً فَتَيَمَّمُوا صَعِيدًا طَيِّبًا فَامْسَحُوا بِوُجُوهِكُمْ وَأَيْدِيكُمْ مِنْهُ" — "and if you do not find water, then betake yourselves to clean earth and wipe your faces and your hands with it." [al-Maidah 6:6] It is related from the People of the House that whoever does not find water, whether a traveler, or one who is unable for it through sickness and fears that washing would destroy him or strengthen his ailment against him, or is simply unable for it, should perform tayammum with the clean earth, which is pure dust. He places the inner surfaces of his palms upon it, then shakes them off with a light shaking, and wipes his face and the backs of his hands with them: he wipes the top of the back of his right hand with the inner surface of his left palm, and the back of his left hand with the inner surface of his right palm. The limit of this is the place from which the hand of the thief is cut off, because Allah Almighty said: "فَامْسَحُوا بِوُجُوهِكُمْ وَأَيْدِيكُمْ مِنْهُ" — "wipe your faces and your hands with it," [al-Maidah 6:6] and He, the Exalted, said: "وَالسَّارِقُ وَالسَّارِقَةُ فَاقْطَعُوا أَيْدِيَهُمَا جَزَاءً بِمَا كَسَبَا" — "As for the male thief and the female thief, cut off their hands as a recompense for what they have earned." [al-Maidah 5:38] So the limit of tayammum matches the limit of cutting, according to the plain sense of the Book of Allah; and the limit of cutting we shall mention in its place, if Allah wills. One begins the wiping from the fingertips.

They said that the one performing tayammum should not do so except at the end of the time of the prayer. When the end of the time has come, he performs tayammum and prays with that tayammum, so long as he does not break his state and does not find water, in which case he performs ablution if he is able for it. If he finds water and is one who is able but does not purify himself, his tayammum is annulled; and when another prayer comes upon him and he still does not find water or is unable for it, he performs tayammum again. If he performs tayammum and then finds water before he prays, he purifies himself and does not pray with his tayammum. If he has already opened the prayer and then finds water, he does not break off the prayer, and it suffices him when this occurs at the end of the time. If he prays having performed tayammum at the beginning of the time, and then reaches water while enough of the prayer's time remains for him to purify himself and pray, he purifies himself and prays. Tayammum is only with dry clean earth, and it is not valid with clay, unless one smears it upon something until it dries and then performs tayammum with it. If he finds no clean earth, he shakes his garment or his felt or his saddle-pad, if there is dust in it, and performs tayammum with that; and one striking suffices him, striking the clean earth with both hands for his face and his hands, once each. Tayammum is not valid while at home, except that a funeral is present and water is not found within the time, for then he performs tayammum and prays over it, if he fears it will be missed. Whoever is on a journey and has with him a little water, and fears that if he purifies himself with it he will perish of thirst, may perform tayammum and keep his water, and not destroy himself. And if he cannot find water except in a place where he fears that going to it would destroy him, he is not obliged to go to it and expose himself to destruction, but performs tayammum and prays. And if he finds water at a dear price, and has the means for its price, he buys it, unless that would cause him ruinous hardship.

When water must be bought, one is not required to pay beyond a fair price when the seller's demand is extortionate, for there is no obligation to meet a ruinous cost. And whoever has no water with him on a journey, and wishes to return to his household, may do so; he performs tayammum and prays.

**Menstruation**

Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said:

﴿وَيَسْأَلُونَكَ عَنِ الْمَحِيضِ قُلْ هُوَ أَذًى فَاعْتَزِلُوا النِّسَاءَ فِي الْمَحِيضِ وَلَا تَقْرَبُوهُنَّ حَتَّى يَطْهُرْنَ فَإِذَا تَطَهَّرْنَ فَأْتُوهُنَّ مِنْ حَيْثُ أَمَرَكُمُ اللَّهُ إِنَّ اللَّهَ يُحِبُّ التَّوَّابِينَ وَيُحِبُّ الْمُتَطَهِّرِينَ﴾

"And they ask you about menstruation; say, It is harm, so keep away from women during menstruation, and do not approach (الْمُبَاشَرَة) them until they are purified. And when they have purified themselves, then come to them as Allah has ordained, for Allah loves those who turn in repentance and loves those who purify themselves." [Al-Baqarah: 222]

This shows that during menstruation one is not in a state of purity. It has been related from the Messenger of Allah (ص) that he said: the menstruating woman is not made pure by water until after the menstruation has ceased.

From the Ahl al-Bayt it is related that the sign of purity for the menstruating woman is that she inserts a clean piece of cotton or cloth, and if it comes out white, she has seen the sign of purity. She then washes with water as she washes from janabah, and she performs the prayers that fall due at that time. She does not make up the prayers she missed during the days of her menstruation. As for the days of her menstruation, she makes up the fast but does not make up the prayer.

Her husband may approach her while she is menstruating if he wishes, though it is better that he keep himself from that. The root of mubasharah (الْمُبَاشَرَة) is the placing of one skin (اَلْبَشَرَة) upon another, and from this the word is derived; it is said that a man "approaches" his wife when their skin touches. But if he has intercourse with her while she is menstruating, he has sinned and transgressed what Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has commanded. He must seek Allah's forgiveness, repent to Him, and give charity for that act; and it is better that he not return to it.

If the bleeding continues beyond what is known of ordinary menstruation, this is istihada. The blood of istihada is distinguished from menstrual blood: menstrual blood is turbid, thick, and foul in odor, while the blood of istihada is thin, clear, and without odor. When the menstrual bleeding stops, the mustahada purifies herself with water, prays, seeks forgiveness, and resumes her ordinary way of life, and her husband may approach her.

It has been related from the Ahl al-Bayt of the Messenger of Allah (ص) that the mustahada should perform ghusl for every prayer, seeking thereby the reward of Allah, Mighty and Majestic; and Allah will record its merit for her if she is sincere in it. If a menstruating woman becomes pure and then sees a faint blood that soon ceases, no ghusl is required of her, for that is like the blood of istihada. But if it is thick, like menstrual blood, she must wash from it. Likewise, if she sees blood while pregnant and it resembles menstruation, then she and her husband must observe in her case what is obligatory during menstruation. She washes when her postpartum bleeding has ended, just as she washes from janabah.

The menstruating woman and the one in a state of janabah may not recite the Quran, nor may they sit in the mosque. And if a menstruating woman becomes pure at the time of a prayer but delays the ghusl until that prayer's time has passed, she is obliged to make up that prayer.

## 3. The Pillar of Religion: Standing in Prayer

Allah Almighty has made prayer a decree of specified times, saying: "Prayer was for the believers a decree of specified times." [Al-Nisa: 103] And He said: "Guard strictly the prayers and the middle prayer, and stand up devoutly before Allah." [Al-Baqarah: 238] And He said: "Indeed, prayer prohibits immorality and wrongdoing, and the remembrance of Allah is greater." [Al-Ankabut: 45] And He said: "And seek help through patience and prayer, and indeed it is difficult except for the humbly submissive." [Al-Baqarah: 45]

> وَأْمُرْ أَهْلَكَ بِٱلصَّلَوٰةِ

The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: "Guard yourselves, and know that the best of your deeds is prayer." He said: "Prayer is the offering of every pious person." He said: "Prayer is half of faith." He said: "Everything has a face, and the face of your religion is prayer." And he said: "Prayer is the pillar of religion and the mainstay of Islam."

He also said: Whoever perfects his ablution, prays well, pays the zakat on his wealth, restrains his anger, imprisons his tongue, extends his kindness to others, seeks forgiveness from his Lord, and gives sincere counsel to the people of my household has fulfilled the realities of faith, and the gates of Paradise stand open for him. He said: The devil remains in awe of the believer so long as he safeguards the five prayers; but when the believer neglects them, the devil grows bold against him and casts him into grave sins.

> إِنَّ ٱلْحَسَنَتِ يُذْهِبْنَ

It is narrated from Ali (ع) that he said: Prayer is the pillar of religion and the mainstay of Islam, and it is the first thing Allah Almighty examines of the deeds of the son of Adam. If it is sound, the rest of his deeds are examined; and if it is not sound, none of his deeds are looked at, and there is no portion in Islam for the one who abandons prayer. He said: The five prayers are an expiation for what falls between them, so long as the major sins are avoided. This is what Allah Almighty meant when He said: "Indeed, good deeds remove evil deeds; that is a reminder for those who remember." [Hud: 114]

He said: The likeness of one who does not complete his prayer is the likeness of a pregnant woman who, when her time of delivery draws near, miscarries, so she is neither one who carries a child nor one who has a child. He said: Two rakahs performed with composure in the latter part of the night are more precious than standing the whole night without composure. And he said: Prayer is a balance; whoever gives it in full receives in full.

Jafar ibn Muhammad (ع) said: I know of nothing after knowledge, meaning knowledge of Allah and His friends, that is superior to prayer, zakat, fasting, Hajj, and jihad. He said: The nearest a servant comes to Allah is when he is in prayer. And he used to say: O seeker of knowledge, pray before you are no longer able, for there will come a time when you can neither pray by night nor by day. The likeness of prayer for the one who offers it is the likeness of a man who is admitted before a sultan and remains silent and attentive until his need is fulfilled. So it is with the Muslim: when he enters into prayer, Allah continues to look upon him until he finishes his prayer.

He was asked about the words of Allah Almighty: "And those who carefully maintain their prayers." [Al-Maarij: 34] He said: This is the obligation. Whoever prays it in its time, knowing its due, preferring nothing over it, Allah Almighty writes for him by it an immunity from the Fire and does not punish him. And whoever prays it outside its time, not knowing its due, preferring other things over it, his affair rests with Allah Almighty: if He wills, He forgives him, and if He wills, He punishes him.

He said: The worshipper has three signs. Mercy showers down upon him from the reaches of heaven to the crown of his head; the angels enfold him from the place of his feet to the reaches of heaven; and a caller calls out to him: "O worshipper, if you knew Whom you address and Who watches you in this prayer, you would not turn away." It is narrated of Ali ibn al-Husayn (ع) that when he rose to pray his color would change and he would tremble, and when he was asked about this he said: "I am about to stand before a great King."

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: The five prayers, whoever safeguards their times and observes their limits will meet Allah on the Day of Resurrection with a covenant with Him that will admit him into Paradise; and whoever squanders their times and does not observe their limits will meet Allah with no covenant with Him: if He wills, He forgives him, and if He wills, He punishes him. It was said to him: What of one who is in his prayer and a need presses upon him in haste? He said: First let him know that the fulfillment of the need lies in the hand of the One before Whom he prays.

He said: The most beloved of deeds to Allah is prayer, and it is the last of the counsels of the prophets. Nothing is more excellent than that a man should wash or perform ablution and complete it, then withdraw to a place where no companion sees him, and Allah looks upon him as he bows and prostrates. When the servant prostrates, Iblis cries: "Woe to me! This one obeyed while I disobeyed; this one prostrated while I refused." He said: The nearest a servant comes to Allah is when he prostrates. And he said: When the Muslim servant devotes himself in his prayer, Allah Almighty turns His face toward him and appoints an angel to gather up the Quran from his mouth. He remains thus so long as he is turned toward his prayer; but if he turns away from it, Allah Almighty turns away from him and consigns him to the angel.

> وَلَهُ ٱلْحَمْدُ فِى ٱلسَّمَوَتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ

As for the times of the prayers, Allah Almighty said: "So exalt Allah when you reach the evening and when you reach the morning, and to Him is all praise in the heavens and the earth, and in the late afternoon and when you reach the time of noon." [Al-Rum: 17-18] And He said: "and after the Isha prayer." [Al-Nur: 58] And the Almighty said: "Establish prayer at the decline of the sun until the darkness of the night, and the Quran of Fajr; indeed, the Quran of Fajr is ever witnessed." [Al-Isra: 78]

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: The decline of the sun is its departure from the zenith, and that is the first time of Zuhr. The darkness of the night is its midpoint, and these encompass four prayers. And the Quran of Fajr is the Fajr prayer. Concerning the words of Allah Almighty, "So exalt Allah when you reach the evening" [Al-Rum: 17], he said: that is the Maghrib and Isha prayers. "And when you reach the morning" [Al-Rum: 17] is the Fajr prayer. "And in the late afternoon" [Al-Rum: 18] is the Asr prayer. "And when you reach the time of noon" [Al-Rum: 18] is the Zuhr prayer. These are the times of the prayers in the Book of Allah Almighty, stated in summary, and the Messenger of Allah ﷺ interpreted and clarified them, as Allah Almighty had commanded him to make plain what was revealed to him.

He said: Jibril came to me when the light of dawn broke across the eastern horizon and stretched to the right and to the left. Dawn is of two kinds. The true dawn is this spreading light, and it is the first time of the Fajr prayer, by which food and all else forbidden to the fasting person become forbidden to him. Before it is the first dawn, called the wolf's tail, a thin light that rises from the place of sunrise and climbs upward without spreading across the horizon as the second dawn does. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: Jibril said to me, "Rise and pray Fajr," so I did. He came to me again on the second day after the light had spread, when the people could see one another, and said to me, "Rise and pray Fajr." He came to me for Zuhr when the sun had passed its zenith and the shadow of everything had become equal to it, meaning after the decline. For Asr, when the shadow of everything had become twice its length. For Maghrib, when the disc had set, meaning when the sun had gone down, and he came at that same time the next day. For the later Isha, when the twilight had vanished. Jafar ibn Muhammad said: this is the redness that lingers on the western horizon after the setting of the sun. On the second night he came when a third of the night had passed. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said that Jibril then said to him: "The time is what lies between your prayer yesterday and your prayer today."

This is a well-attested report from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ, which we have set down in brief. It occurred at Makkah, for the report states that Jibril (ع) came to him there; then the Messenger of Allah ﷺ later established at Madinah a Sunnah concerning the times. Many reports on this have been narrated from the Imams of his household (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), and from many others among the general body of Muslims as well, and I mention here only what suffices within the scope I have set for this book.

What we have narrated from the Imams of the household of the Messenger of Allah ﷺ is that every prayer has two times, except the Maghrib prayer, whose time is the setting of the sun and is not to be delayed beyond it. The most excellent times are their beginnings, save the later Isha, for its merit lies in delaying it, on account of the hardship in this for one who wishes to sleep, since the one who awaits the prayer is himself in prayer, as has come from the Messenger of Allah ﷺ. The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: One of you prays the prayer, and what has passed him of its time is a graver loss to him than to be deprived of his family and his wealth.

Ali said: Render the rights of your Lord at their due moments; pray the prayer at the first of its times. He said: The first of the time is the most excellent, so hasten to good as much as you are able. He said: The first of the time is the pleasure of Allah, and the end of the time is the pardon of Allah, and pardon is only for a sin or a shortcoming. And Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali (ع) said: The first of the time is ever the more excellent, so hasten to the merit as much as you are able. He said: The first of the time is the time of Allah, and it is the better of the two. He said: The first of the time is the most excellent, and what follows it remains open until the end of the time.

It is narrated from Jafar ibn Muhammad that he said: It is not for anyone to take the later of the two times as his time except for an infirmity or an excuse; the later time is only for one who is occupied, or forgets, or sleeps, for it was made for the sick and the one driven to it by necessity. He said: When the sun declines, the gates of heaven are opened, and I do not wish anyone to outstrip me in works, for I love that my scroll be the first scroll in which a righteous deed is written. And he said: How can one of you feel secure from the sudden calamities that may befall him if he neglects the prayer when its time has already entered?

We have narrated from the Ahl al-Bayt (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) that they enjoined watching for the zawal, which is the sun's departure from the middle of the sky toward the west. People have devised many instruments to determine this, the soundest and most reliable being that a circle is drawn with a compass upon a board or tile fixed in a place that catches the sun, laid perfectly level so that no edge rises above another. This is tested by pouring water upon it: if one side sinks, the water runs to it, and it is adjusted until level, then fastened, and the circle is drawn upon it. In its center, at the point where the compass foot stood, a small even nail is fixed, of a height such that the tip of its shadow reaches the line of the circle at the time of the sun. Then one observes at the start of day: when the tip of the nail's shadow reaches the line of the circle on the western side, that place is marked on the line with a point. In the late afternoon one likewise observes where the shadow reaches the line of the circle and marks it. Then with the compass one measures the arc between the two points on the southern side and marks its middle, and does the same on the northern side and marks it. A line is drawn along a straight edge from the southern mark to the northern mark, passing through the center of the nail, and that line lies opposite the middle of the sky. When the shadow lies straight along it, the sun stands at the middle of the sky; and when the shadow inclines from it a little, the sun has declined, and that is the first time of Zuhr, when one prays the Sunnah and then the obligatory prayer.

Ali said: The prayer of the decline is the prayer of the oft-returning. Jafar ibn Muhammad said: At that hour the gates of heaven are opened, supplication is answered, the winds stir, and Allah looks upon His creation.

We have narrated from the Ahl al-Bayt that when the sun declines, the time of the two prayers, Zuhr and Asr, enters. Rabia agreed with this saying, holding that the time of Zuhr and Asr, whether traveling or at home, is when the sun declines. Ibn al-Mubarak expressed a view close to it, saying: If a man were to rise when the shadow of everything equals its length after the decline and pray Zuhr, and another were to pray Asr at that same time, each would have prayed his prayer in its time. It was said to him: How can there be one time for two prayers without travel or excuse? Ibn al-Mubarak said to the questioner: Does this trouble you? It is Jibril who brought it; had he brought a single time for three prayers, we would have made it so for them.

The time of the Asr prayer, according to the Ahl al-Bayt, is after the worshipper has finished the Zuhr prayer, its obligatory and its Sunnah. The most they mentioned for this is about two hours of the day, so that the Asr prayer falls at the beginning of the ninth hour, and upon this is the practice; it ought not to be delayed. If it is delayed for an excuse or infirmity, the end of its time is the yellowing of the sun. The Maghrib prayer, its time is the setting of the sun below the western horizon, and it has no other time, as already mentioned. The times of the later Isha and of Fajr are as set out before. The rising of the sun is known by its rising itself; and if a mountain or some obstacle stands before its place of rising, the sign of its rising is the reddening of the western horizon, and the sign of its setting is the darkening of the eastern horizon. The night prayer follows the later Isha, and Witr follows the night prayer; there is no prayer after Witr until dawn rises, when one prays the two rakahs of Fajr and then Fajr after them.

They forbade the supererogatory prayer after sunrise until the sun has climbed, unless one enters the mosque and prays two rakahs before sitting; and likewise no supererogatory prayer is offered after Asr until the sun sets, though there is no harm in making up an obligatory prayer at these two times. They forbade prayer at the moment of the sun's rising and at its setting. When it is feared that an obligatory prayer will be lost, one begins with it before the supererogatory. The Zuhr prayer is delayed a little in intense heat, past the decline, to seek some coolness, for those who pray in congregation in a mosque. The Friday prayer is prayed when the sun declines and should not be delayed.

Zuhr and Asr, and Maghrib and Isha, may be joined at the end of the time of the first of them, while traveling, if there is need; and in residence, in the mosques, when there is an excuse such as rain, wind, or darkness. They are joined with one adhan and two iqamahs: when the imam finishes the first prayer he remains in his place, the muezzin rises and gives the iqamah, then the imam rises and prays the second.

Whoever forgets a prayer and does not recall it until the end of the time of a prayer now due, and fears that if he begins with the one he has missed he will lose the one whose time he is in, let him begin with the present one. But if he knows he can make up the missed prayer and still catch the present one, he begins with the missed one. If many prayers have escaped him, he begins with those he knows he can make up while still reaching the prayer whose time he is in, and leaves the rest to make up afterward. Whoever forgets a prayer and does not recall it until he has already stood in another prayer: if the one he forgot was Zuhr and he did not recall it until he had entered Asr, he forms the intention of Zuhr and then makes up Asr; and if it is otherwise, he completes the prayer he is in and then makes up what escaped him. Abu Abd Allah Jafar ibn Muhammad was asked about the difference between these two cases, and he said: because the Asr prayer has no prayer after it. Whoever misses a supererogatory prayer may make it up if he wishes, and in that there is merit, though it is not obligatory. And whoever prays a prayer before its time has not discharged it, and must repeat it after its time has entered.

Once the worshipper has attained the purity that prayer requires, what summons him to stand before his Lord is the call to prayer, and the place appointed for that standing is the mosque. Of both the tradition has much to say.

### The Call to Prayer

Muhammad ibn Ali was asked about the origin of the call to prayer. He said that the Messenger of Allah (ص) was taught it on the night of al-Isra. He recounted the hadith of al-Isra at length: that the angel called the adhan to the Messenger of Allah (ص), and that he led the Prophets (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) in prayer.

He was then told that some people claimed the call to prayer had arisen from a vision seen by a man of the Ansar. At this he grew very angry and said, "إِنَّا لِلَّهِ وَإِنَّآ إِلَيْهِ رَجِعُونَ. To Allah we belong and to Him we shall return. They have set their hand to the very face of their religion and fixed it upon a vision that a man among the people saw, as though the Messenger of Allah (ص) had followed him in it." Yet Allah, Mighty and Majestic, had said to His Messenger: "Say: I follow only what is revealed to me."

The Messenger of Allah (ص) said, "There are three things which, if my ummah knew what reward lay in them, they would draw lots to attain them: the call to prayer, going early to the Friday prayer, and the first row." It was said to him, "O Messenger of Allah, do you not fear that your nation will contend over this after you, even with swords, when they hear it?" He said, "Only the weak among them will stoop to contend over it."

> وَلَوْ تَرَىٰٓ إِذِ ٱلْمُجْرِمُونَ نَاكِسُوا۟ رُءُوسِهِمْ عِندَ رَبِّهِمْ

He said, "The muezzins will be raised on the Day of Resurrection as the longest-necked of people, calling out the testimony that there is no god but Allah." By the length of their necks that day he (ص) meant their yearning toward the mercy of their Lord and their security from His punishment, in contrast to the state in which Allah, Mighty and Majestic, described the guilty when He said: "And if you could see the criminals bowing their heads before their Lord" [al-Sajdah: 12]. This is a known usage in the speech of the Arabs, who say of a man visited by something he detests that his head has fallen low.

Ali said, "I grieve over nothing except that I wish I had asked the Messenger of Allah (ص) for the adhan for al-Hasan and al-Husayn."

We have narrated from the Ahl al-Bayt that they commanded the adhan be given with measured deliberation and the iqamah briskly; that both be given in paired phrases, with the final testimony of the iqamah pronounced singly; and that the adhan be given with "حَيَّ عَلَى خَيْرِ الْعَمَلِ, Come to the best of deeds," and the iqamah likewise.

"Come to the best of deeds" was part of the call to prayer in the days of the Messenger of Allah (ص), of Abu Bakr, and in the first part of Umar's days. Then Umar saw fit to strike it from the call, saying, "If the ignorant hear that prayer is the best of deeds, they will abandon jihad." This has been related by many of the general body of scholars. But Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has set out the merit of prayer in more than one place in His Book, and set out the merit of jihad in it as well; and what may befall jihad is no reason to cut away what Allah has commanded and what the Sunnah of His Messenger (ص) established. Rather, the ignorant ought to be taught the truth of which they were ignorant and brought to see it clearly, so that their ignorance of a thing is not made a cause for dropping it. Were such a thing warranted, most of the obligations would fall away, for people are ignorant of most of them and confused about them in general.

They said that the muezzin, when he gives the adhan, should stand facing the qiblah. When he reaches "حَيَّ عَلَى الصَّلَاةِ, Come to prayer," he turns his face to the right; and when he reaches "حَيَّ عَلَى الْفَلَاحِ, Come to success," he turns his face to the left, keeping his feet in their place. There is no harm in a traveler giving the adhan while mounted, but he should give the iqamah only while standing on the ground. He should not give the adhan unless he is in a state of purity; if he gives it without purity, there is no harm, but he must not lead the prayer except in purity.

When the Messenger of Allah (ص) heard the muezzin, he would repeat what the muezzin said, until, reaching "Come to prayer," "Come to success," and "Come to the best of deeds," he would say, "لَا حَوْلَ وَلَا قُوَّةَ إِلَّا بِالله, There is no power and no strength except with Allah." He said that whoever says this seeking the pleasure of Allah has from Allah a covenant that the Fire shall never touch him.

When the muezzin says that the prayer is now ready, it becomes obligatory upon those present in the mosque to fall silent and rise for prayer; there is no harm in speaking before that. The muezzin should not break off the adhan with speech or anything else; but if ignorance or necessity compels him to speak, let him resume the adhan from where he left off, and nothing further is required of him. The same holds for the iqamah. The adhan and the iqamah are to be given in every mosque in which prayer is performed.

It is not fitting to omit them, or either of them. Whoever prays alone should still give the adhan and the iqamah. Abu Jafar ibn Muhammad said, "Whoever gives the adhan and the iqamah and then prays has two rows of angels praying behind him; and whoever gives the iqamah without the adhan and prays has one row of angels behind him." To give both the adhan and the iqamah for every prayer is better. Whoever gives the iqamah without the adhan, that suffices him; and whoever prays with neither adhan nor iqamah need not repeat the prayer, for the call of the town suffices him. Neither adhan nor iqamah is required of women; if they give them, it is good and meritorious, and if they content themselves with the call of the town, that too is permitted them.

Whoever forgets the adhan and the iqamah, or one of them, until he has entered the prayer, owes nothing; he continues his prayer and does not break it off, for the prayer is an obligation and the adhan is a Sunnah, and an obligatory prayer is not to be broken for a Sunnah whose place is fulfilled without it, as we have said. And if, before entering the prayer, he is uncertain whether he gave the adhan or the iqamah, not knowing whether he did so or not, then if he gives them it is good, and if he prays without them nothing is required of him.

The adhan should not be given before the time of the prayer has come in. Whoever does so must repeat it once the time enters; if he does not repeat it, nothing is required of him, and his case is that of one who prays without the adhan. They permitted the adhan for the Fajr prayer alone to be given before dawn, though the adhan at dawn itself is better; the earlier call was permitted in order to rouse the sleeper. Confusion may arise on this account for one ignorant of the dawn, who prays when he hears the adhan before the time of prayer, so that his prayer does not count for him. The adhan at its proper time is better, and it is the practice that the Messenger of Allah (ص) established; upon it the imams call to prayer today, the muezzin greeting the imam and announcing the prayer to him after the adhan.

They permitted the adhan and iqamah to be given by a slave or by a boy who has not yet reached the age of dreams, and that the prayer be led in that manner. When the muezzin gives the adhan on behalf of the mosque and remains, the adhan and the iqamah stand for every prayer performed in that mosque; and those who were in the mosque when the adhan was given should not leave until they have prayed, unless one goes out for a need intending to return, knowing that he will still reach the prayer, or unless one is without ablution and goes out to perform it, striving to return in time. Even if he does not reach it, he may pray alone or join another congregation in another mosque, and no blame falls upon him.

There is no harm in giving the adhan in the mosque before the imam arrives, even at the very beginning of the time. If the appointed imam of the mosque comes, none other should be put forward to lead them, for he is an imam installed by the Imam; he waits with the prayer until the end of the time, and if he comes, well and good, and if not, they pray for themselves. Among the people of a locality, when they dispute over who should give the adhan, the worthiest, if they are equal in merit, is the one with the loudest voice. There is no harm in the adhan of a blind man if he follows the adhan of another who knows the time. There is no adhan or iqamah for a supererogatory prayer. A muezzin should not climb a minaret so high that he looks down from it upon the private quarters of the people and sees into their homes.

The Messenger of Allah (ص) said, "Whoever has a child born to him, let him give the adhan in the child's right ear and the iqamah in the left, and let him recite the Opening of the Book, the Verse of the Throne, the end of Surat al-Hashr, Surat al-Ikhlas, and the two surahs of refuge." He said, "Whoever does this for his child, the child is guarded from the accursed devil and his licking." The licking is when the devil licks the child's tongue: he touches the newborn with his tongue to defile it with his filth, and whoever does these things for the child guards him from it. The Messenger of Allah (ص) also said, "If the desert demons beset you, give the call to prayer." The ghilan are the saali, and they are called the enchanters of the jinn; and his words, that the desert demons beset you, mean that the road has led you astray. So the Messenger of Allah (ص) commanded that whoever strays from the road and does not know it should give the call to prayer, for it will guide him, if Allah wills.

### On the Mosques

Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: "The mosques of Allah are maintained only by those who believe in Allah and the Last Day, and establish prayer, and give the zakat, and fear none but Allah; it may be that those will be among the rightly guided" [al-Tawbah: 18]. And He, Exalted, said: "And the places of worship are for Allah alone, so call not upon anyone alongside Allah" [al-Jinn: 18]. And He, Exalted, said: "The polytheists are but impurity, so let them not approach the Sacred Mosque after this year of theirs" [al-Tawbah: 28].

The Messenger of Allah (ص) said, "Do not set out on a journey to any mosque save three: the Sacred Mosque, my mosque, and the mosque of Jerusalem." He said that prayer in the Sacred Mosque is equal to a hundred thousand prayers; prayer in the mosque of Medina to ten thousand; prayer in the mosque of Jerusalem to a thousand; prayer in the great mosque, meaning the mosque in which the Friday prayer is gathered in each region, to a hundred; prayer in the mosque of one's tribe to twenty-five; prayer in the market mosque to twelve; and a man's prayer alone in his house to a single prayer. He said that sitting in the mosque to await the prayer is itself an act of worship. He said, "Whoever takes the Quran as his companion and the mosque as his home, Allah builds for him a house in Paradise and raises him a degree below the middle station." And he said that the believer's place is his mosque, and his home is his hermitage.

He forbade the uncovering of the private parts in the mosque, that being what lies between the navel and the knee. He forbade that the prescribed punishments be carried out in the mosques, meaning retaliation for killing, and stoning, and the penalties for adultery, for false accusation, and for drinking; but as for matters of correction and discretionary discipline, there is no harm in their being addressed in the mosque. He forbade the raising of voices in the mosque, the drawing of weapons in it, buying and selling in it, the crying of lost property in it, and the sitting in it of one in a state of janabah.

It was narrated from Ali that he said, "There is no prayer for a neighbor of the mosque except in the mosque." He was asked, "And who is a neighbor of the mosque?" He said, "Whoever hears the call," meaning the adhan. This, and Allah knows best, is by way of stern admonition and discipline, for the prayer of one who lives near the mosque yet prays in his house is defective and he is bound to repeat it; yet the merit lies in praying in the mosque. It may be that his saying "there is no prayer" means that it has no prayer worthy of the name, that is, no prayer possessing the merit of one prayed in the mosque; and we have already spoken of the multiplication of reward for prayer in the mosque.

It was narrated from the Messenger of Allah (ص) that a man asked him, "O Messenger of Allah, I go out to my camels with my family, my children, and my servant-boys, and I give the adhan and the iqamah and lead them in prayer; are we a congregation, with the merit of one?" He said, "Yes." The man said, "Sometimes the servant-boys go off to graze the camels, so I give the adhan and the iqamah and lead my family and my children in prayer; are we a congregation?" He said, "Yes." The man said, "Sometimes the children go off tracking the traces of rain, so I give the adhan and the iqamah and pray with my family; are we a congregation?" He said, "Yes." The man said, "Sometimes the household is occupied with its labor, so I give the adhan and the iqamah and pray alone; am I a congregation?" The Messenger of Allah (ص) said, "The believer alone is a congregation."

We have mentioned before that whoever gives the adhan and the iqamah and prays has two rows of angels praying behind him. What is commended for the worshipper who intends to pray is that he pray in the great mosque, for the multiplied reward that has been transmitted concerning prayer in it, as we have mentioned. If he cannot, let him pray in the mosque of his tribe; if that is not possible, let him pray in congregation in a market or wherever a congregation may be formed; and if he cannot do even that, let him pray in his house and gather his family and his children with him, for in that lies the merit of congregation and the reward of keeping his household constant in prayer. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: "And enjoin the prayer upon your household, and be steadfast in it" [Taha: 132]. And if he cannot do that, let him give the adhan and the iqamah and pray alone; and we have mentioned the merit of that and how the reward is lessened, degree by degree.

Ali said, "Waiting for the prayer after the prayer is a kind of frontier-guarding." And it is of the Sunnah, when you sit in the mosque, to face the qiblah, that is, when one is able; and if he turns aside from it, there is no harm. He said, "When the afflictions descend, the people of the mosques are kept safe." And he said, "Reclining at ease in the mosques is the monasticism of the Arabs."

The Messenger of Allah (ص) said, "Keep your mosques clear of your buying and selling, your disputes, your quarrels, your weapons, and your madmen"; and he forbade the raising of voices in them and the carrying out of the prescribed punishment. He said that they were to be perfumed with incense every seven days, and that the means of purification were to be placed in them.

Whoever keeps the mosque clear of his spittle will meet Allah on the Day of Resurrection with a smiling face, his record given into his right hand; for the mosque recoils from spittle as a morsel of flesh recoils in the fire, and writhes at it as one of you writhes from the stroke of a cane. Spitting in the mosque is a sin, and its expiation is to bury it; and whoever spits in the mosque, let him pray two rakahs.

He said, concerning the mosque and what is forbidden within it, that the prohibition falls upon whoever casts something onto the wall of the mosque, its floor, or its carpet.
