These are: the fast of Rajab; the fast of some days of Shawwal after Eid al-Fitr, during which the reward of Ramadan is extended; the fast of nine days of Dhu al-Hijjah, which are the days of the ten; the fast of the day of Ashura by refraining from food and drink without holding the belief that such a fast is superior, for that abstention resembles mourning more than devotion; the fast of the month of Muharram, for it is one of the sacred months; the fast of the sick person and the traveler, since upon them lies the making up of a number of other days, whether they fasted or broke; the fast of Monday; and the fast of permission, which includes that a woman may not fast voluntarily except with her husband's permission, a bondsman may not fast voluntarily except with his master's permission, and a guest may not fast voluntarily except with his host's permission.

Among the fasts, too, is the fast of withholding speech, of which Allah Almighty informed us in the account of Maryam, in His words: *"Eat and drink and be comforted; and if you see any human being, say: I have vowed to the Most Merciful a fast, so I will not speak this day to any human."* He commanded her to eat, to drink, and to speak, by His words *"so say: I have vowed,"* and this indicates that the speech she was forbidden was some speech and not all of it, just as it is permitted to speak within the prayer with the intimate supplication of Allah Almighty while all else is withheld from it, as I have mentioned in the chapters on prayer.

The Messenger of Allah (ص) did not bind himself to a prayer that was not prescribed; yet the Imams of his progeny bound themselves to certain prayers and commanded their followers to observe them by way of encouragement and persuasion, not as a binding obligation that may not be left, and not as though it were like the obligatory prayer. In the same way, the Messenger of Allah (ص) bound himself, in the latter part of his life, to fasting two months in the year besides the month of Ramadan; and likewise the Imams of his progeny bound themselves to that and urged their followers to it by way of exhortation and encouragement, not as something binding that may not be abandoned.

It has been narrated from the Imams of his progeny that the Messenger of Allah (ص) would fast until it was said of him that he does not break his fast, and then break his fast until it was said of him that he does not fast. He used to fast Muharram, Rajab, and Shaban; he used to fast one day and break the fast the next; he used to fast Monday and Thursday; and he used to fast the white nights, which are the thirteenth of the month and the two days after it. These are called the white nights because the moon appears in them from the beginning of the night to its end, as though they were made white by its light. Then his practice came to this: that he would fast Shaban and three days in every month, namely a Thursday at the beginning of the month, which is its first Thursday, a Thursday at its end, which is its last Thursday, and a Wednesday in its middle, the Wednesday nearest to the midpoint from either side. Those two months are the like of the obligatory fast in reward, and upon this practice he (ص) was taken.

It has been narrated from Abu Abdullah Jafar ibn Muhammad that he would fast Rajab, Shaban, and Ramadan, joining them together. He said that Nuh (ع) boarded the Ark on the first day of Rajab and commanded those with him of the jinn and mankind to fast it. He said: whoever fasts a month of Rajab, the Fire is set from him the distance of a year's journey; if he fasts it two years, the Fire recedes from him the distance of two years; and so it continues, until when he fasts it seven, the seven gates of the Fire are shut against him; if he fasts it eight, the eight gates of Paradise are opened to him; if he fasts it ten, his request is granted; if he fasts it fifteen, it is said to him, "Begin your works anew"; and whoever adds more, Allah adds to him.

Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali was asked about the fast of the day of Ashura and said that it was before the fast of Ramadan was made obligatory. Abu Abdullah Jafar ibn Muhammad said that the Ark came to rest upon Mount Judi on the day of Ashura, and so Nuh commanded those with him to fast that day. He said that it is the day on which Allah Almighty accepted the repentance of Adam and Hawwa; the day on which the sea was parted for the Children of Israel and Pharaoh was drowned; the day on which Ibrahim (ع) was born; the day on which Allah Almighty accepted the repentance of the people of Yunus (ع); the day on which Isa ibn Maryam (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) was born; the day on which Allah Almighty honored al-Husayn (ع) with martyrdom; and the day on which the Qaim from his progeny shall rise.

Imam al-Muizz li-Din Allah (ع) said that the day of Ashura is a day that Allah, His Messenger, and His friends have magnified, for the merit and the proof it contains; while the enemies of Allah's friends, from the Umayyads and the progeny of Marwan, magnified it for what they attained in it in violating the sanctities of Allah and the religion of Allah and killing the sons of the Messenger of Allah (ص). So they set it in the place of gladness and gloating rather than the place of seeking forgiveness and righteous works, turning aside from the Sunnah of Allah and the Sunnah of His Prophet regarding it, and persisting in the things Allah has forbidden and in disobedience to Him.

It has come from the Imams that they held it beloved to fast the day of Arafah, for whoever knows its virtue, except for one who is at the standing place and fears that the fast would weaken him for the standing. And they disliked singling out Friday for fasting, unless it is fasted together with another day, before it or after it.

Beyond the zakat that seals the fast, the fast itself carries its own fine rulings, and the Imams drew careful lines around when a person fasts, when he refrains, and when fasting is not permitted at all.

Ali said, "Whoever fasts on Friday, it is as if he fasted for the period between the two Fridays," meaning that a day of the week arrives and he joins it to another rather than singling Friday out for fasting by itself. He also said that he would not accept from anyone who had an obligatory fast still outstanding that he perform a supererogatory fast until the obligation upon him is first discharged. Yet a concession was granted in the other direction: whoever has begun a voluntary fast may break it, and no making-up is required of him, provided the sun has not yet passed its zenith. Should he break the voluntary fast after the sun has declined, he must then make up that day. And they forbade fasting on the day of al-Fitr, on the day of al-Adha, and on the three days of al-Tashreeq. They likewise forbade al-wasal in fasting, which is that the fasting person joins two days or more together without breaking his fast between them.

> أُحِلَّ لَكُمْ لَيْلَةَ ٱلصِّيَامِ ٱلرَّفَثُ إِلَىٰ نِسَآئِكُمْ

From fasting the teaching turns to itikaf, the retreat that fasting makes possible. Itikaf is the act of remaining and residing within a mosque, the akif being the one who stays there. Of it Allah, mighty and majestic, said, "the one who remains there and the visitor are equal," and He, blessed and exalted, said, "And do not engage in relations with them while you are in retreat in the mosques" [al-Baqarah: 187] ﴿ وَلَا تُبَاشِرُوهُنَّ وَأَنْتُمْ عَاكِفُونَ فِى الْمَسَاجِدِ﴾, the reference being to women. The Messenger of Allah, (ع), used to observe itikaf in the last ten days of Ramadan, binding himself to the mosque by night and by day, and he used to say that itikaf in those ten days is equal in reward to two Hajj and two Umrahs. Through those nights he would remain standing in prayer, and each night he would bathe between the two evening prayers. He observed itikaf in the first ten days of Ramadan, then in the middle ten in the following year, and then in the last ten in the year after that.

Ali said that whoever undertakes itikaf ought to keep to the mosque night and day and give himself over within it to prayer, to the recitation of the Quran, and to the remembrance of Allah, mighty and majestic. He should not occupy himself with conversation except in what cannot be avoided; he should neither sell nor buy, nor recite poetry, nor attend a funeral, nor visit the sick, nor enter a house, nor seclude himself with a woman, nor speak in coarse or whispered speech, nor lay a hand upon anyone. Whatever he holds back from speaking with people is better for him.

It was narrated from Jafar ibn Muhammad that he said, "There is no itikaf except with fasting, and no itikaf except in a mosque in which the congregational prayer is held. The one in itikaf does not pray in his house, does not receive women, does not sell and does not buy, and does not leave the mosque except for a need he cannot forgo, and once he leaves for it he does not sit until he returns." The same rulings hold for a woman in itikaf, except that should she begin to menstruate her itikaf is nullified and she departs from the mosque. And the least duration of itikaf is three days.

## 8. The Journey to the House: Pilgrimage

> وَأَذِّن فِى ٱلنَّاسِ بِٱلْحَجِّ يَأْتُوكَ رِجَالًۭا وَعَلَىٰ كُلِّ ضَامِرٍۢ يَأْتِينَ مِن كُلِّ فَجٍّ عَمِيقٍۢ

There remains the greatest of the journeys undertaken for the sake of Allah, the Hajj, and what is stated concerning it. Allah Almighty said: "And proclaim the Hajj among the people; they will come to you on foot and on every lean camel; they will come from every distant mountain pass" [al-Hajj: 27]. It was narrated from Jafar ibn Muhammad (ع), from his father, that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: When Allah Almighty commanded Ibrahim (ع) to call the people to Hajj, he ascended Mount Safa, placed his finger in his ear, and called out as Allah Almighty had commanded him. His call was heard by those in the east and in the west, even those yet unborn in the loins of their fathers, and they answered him. Whoever fulfills the Hajj once has answered once, whoever fulfills it twice has answered twice, and whoever fulfills it more times has answered accordingly. Jafar ibn Muhammad said: "From this the Talbiyah was made."

The Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: "Hajj is the jihad of every weak person." He also said: "Whoever circumambulates this House seven times and prays two rakahs, the best of prayers, will be forgiven." When he stood on the eve of Arafah, he faced the people and said three times: "Welcome to the delegation of Allah!" Then he said: "Those who ask shall be given, and those who fall short in this world shall be compensated; for every thousand dirhams spent, good deeds are written for them. O people, shall I not give you glad tidings? This evening Allah Almighty commanded His angels, and they descended to the earth, and He said to them: O My angels, look upon My servants, dusty and disheveled, who have come to Me from the ends of the earth. Do you know what they ask? They will say: Our Lord, You know best. And He will say: They ask Me for forgiveness, and I bear witness to you that I have forgiven them."

Hajj carries the reward of the Garden, and Umrah is expiation for every sin. He (ع) said: "Alternate between Hajj and Umrah, for they negate poverty and sins and bring sustenance." He said: "There are sins that cannot be forgiven except at Arafat." Concerning the Hajj caravan he said: "Do not turn them away," meaning that their reward is written for them whether or not you conceal it; no veil is lifted from them unless their evil deeds are removed; and when they have fulfilled their rites it is said to them: "You have built a foundation, so do not demolish it. What has passed is enough for you, so do well in what is to come." The Prophet ﷺ said: "Whoever desires this world and the next, let him come to this House, for whatever a servant has asked of his Lord, Allah will grant him some of it or store it for him." Then he said: "O people, you must perform Hajj and Umrah and alternate between them, for they wash away sins as water washes away grime, and they negate poverty as fire removes the dross of iron."

Ali (ع) used to say that the righteous man, once he has completed the Hajj, takes up his works anew, meaning that he has already been forgiven for what preceded. Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali (ع) traced back to the Prophet ﷺ that he said: "Pilgrims are of three kinds, and the best of them in portion is the man who has been forgiven for his past and future sins. Next is the man who has been forgiven for his past sins and begins his works afresh. Next, and the least of them, is the man who has merely safeguarded his family and his wealth." The pilgrim must inevitably sustain some loss in his wealth, whether through the exertion itself or through a shortfall in his provisioning. Whoever suffers loss in either his wealth or his family while on Hajj has forfeited one of the two rewards, yet he is forgiven for what he has done and what he has deferred, his former sins are forgiven, and he is summoned to fear Allah in what lies ahead.

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: When Allah Almighty sent Adam (ع) down to the earth, he wandered to the House just as he had seen the angels circling the Throne, and he called out to his Lord asking for forgiveness. The call came: "Allah has forgiven you." He said: "O Lord, and my descendants?" And the call came: "O Adam, whoever among your descendants comes to this place bearing his sin as you have borne yours, Allah will forgive him." Jafar ibn Muhammad said: "The guarantee of the believing pilgrim rests with Allah Almighty, that even if he sins after returning to his family, he remains under Allah's protection for seventy nights." He said concerning the words of Allah Almighty, "Purify My House for those who circumambulate it, those who remain in devotion, and those who bow and prostrate" [al-Baqarah: 125]: "When Allah Almighty revealed that, He sent down upon the Kabah one hundred and seventy mercies: sixty for those who circumambulate it, fifty for those who remain in devotion, forty for those who worship within it, and twenty for those who behold it."

A man asked Abu Abdullah Jafar ibn Muhammad: "Which is better, voluntary Hajj or voluntary charity?" The questioner kept recalling charity and its virtue, and Abu Abdullah (ع) kept answering that Hajj is better, repeating it several times, until he looked toward the mountain of Abu Qubays and said: "Even if you were to give in charity its weight in gold and silver, you would not attain the reward of Hajj." Then he said: "Is there in charity a circumambulation of the House? Is there in charity a standing at Arafah? Is there in charity anything of the rites?" The questioner said no. He said: "Then Hajj is better."

He said concerning the words of Allah Almighty, "That they may witness benefits for them" [al-Hajj: 28], that these are benefits for them in their religion. He said: "When the believing servant sets out from his home as a pilgrim, he takes no step, and his mount carries him no stage, except that a good deed is written for him by it, an evil deed is erased from him by it, and he is raised a degree by it. When he stands at Arafah, though his sins were as the number of the dust of the earth, they are erased from him. When he casts the pebbles, his rank is raised, to be fulfilled for him on the Day of Resurrection. When he shaves his head, not a hair falls from him but Allah makes its place a light for him on the Day of Resurrection. Whatever he spends is written for him. And when he circumambulates the House, he returns as the day his mother bore him," meaning sound and free of sins. Then he recited the words of Allah Almighty: "Whoever hastens his departure in two days, there is no sin on him, and whoever delays, there is no sin on him, for the one who fears Allah" [al-Baqarah: 203]. And he said: "No one stands upon those mountains, whether righteous or wicked, and calls upon Allah except that He answers him. As for the righteous, he is answered in his Hereafter, and as for the other, he is answered in this world."

> حِجُّ ٱلْبَيْتِ مَنِ ٱسْتَطَاعَ إِلَيْهِ سَبِيلًۭا ۚ وَمَن كَفَرَ فَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ غَنِىٌّ عَنِ ٱلْعَلَمِينَ

On the obligation of Hajj, Allah Almighty said: "And Hajj to the House is a duty owed to Allah by the people, for whoever is able to find a way to it; and whoever disbelieves, then indeed Allah is free of need of the worlds" [Al Imran: 97]. The Prophet ﷺ said: When this verse was revealed, the people asked: "O Messenger of Allah, is it every year?" He remained silent. They asked him twice more, and he said: "No; and had I said yes, it would have become obligatory." Then Allah Almighty revealed: "O you who believe, do not ask about things that, if disclosed to you, would distress you" [al-Maidah: 101]. Ali said concerning the words of Allah Almighty, "And whoever disbelieves, then indeed Allah is free of need of the worlds" [Al Imran: 97]: "This is said of the one who abandons Hajj while he is able to perform it."

Jafar ibn Muhammad was asked about the words of Allah Almighty, "for whoever is able to find a way to it" [Al Imran: 97]: what is the ability to find a way? He asked the questioner what people say about it, and the man replied that they say it is provisions and a mount. Abu Abdullah said: "Abu Jafar was asked about this, and he said: The people would be ruined then. If a man who has nothing but provisions and a mount, with no sustenance for his family besides that, were compelled to set out with it and not leave it for them, they would indeed be ruined." When asked, then, what the ability is, he said: "The ability to make the journey, sufficient provision for it, the presence of what sustains one's dependents, and the security of the road. Has not Allah Almighty made zakat obligatory only upon the one who possesses two hundred dirhams?"

He was asked about the man who postpones the Hajj, prevented from it only by a trade that occupies him or a debt he owes, and he said: "He has no excuse; it is not fitting for him to postpone the Hajj, and if he dies, he has abandoned one of the laws of Islam." He said: "Whoever dies without having performed the Hajj of Islam, not prevented by a need that pressed hard upon him, nor by an illness he could not bear the journey with, nor by a ruler who barred him, let him die a Jew or a Christian." He said: "Hajj is obligatory upon a person once in his lifetime." It was said to him: "And whoever abandons it while able, until he dies?" He said: "He is among those of whom Allah Almighty said, And We will gather him on the Day of Resurrection blind." It was said: "Blind?" He said: "Yes, blind to the path of truth and good." He said that the Messenger of Allah ﷺ said: "If my ummah were to abandon this House and cease to make for it, they would not be granted respite."

He was asked about the child taken on Hajj, and he said: "That does not suffice for him; the Hajj is upon him when he reaches maturity." He was asked about one who performs Hajj while not knowing this matter, then Allah favors him with its knowledge, and he said: "His Hajj suffices him, though it would be more beloved to me that he perform Hajj again. And if he was a Nasib holding to enmity and performed Hajj upon that, then Allah favored him with knowledge, the Hajj is upon him when he is able." He said of the owned servant who performs Hajj: "It suffices for him so long as he is owned; and if he is freed, the Hajj is upon him when he is able, for there is no obligation of Hajj upon him while he is owned." Likewise the mother of a child whose master takes her on Hajj: it does not suffice for her. A man may not prevent his wife from the obligatory Hajj if she finds a mahram of hers to travel with her, and provision to spend, and the road is secure. He said: "The divorced woman may perform Hajj during her waiting period if she wishes." If a man is insolvent and another performs Hajj on his behalf, then he becomes able, the Hajj is still upon him.

> وَلَا تَحْلِقُوا۟ رُءُوسَكُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَبْلُغَ ٱلْهَدْىُ مَحِلَّهُۥ

He was asked about Umrah and said: "It is obligatory as the Hajj is obligatory," and he recited the words of Allah Almighty: "And complete the Hajj and Umrah for Allah" [al-Baqarah: 196]. He said: "Their completion is their performance." He said that a man may bring forward whichever of the two he wishes, the Hajj or the Umrah, and whoever wishes may join them together. Whoever joins them and drives the offering does not leave the state of ihram until he has sacrificed, and that is the qarin, because Allah Almighty says: "Do not shave your heads until the offering reaches its place." And whoever has no offering with him and joins them is performing tamattu: when he comes to Mecca he performs tawaf and say and leaves ihram, and that is the Umrah; then he enters ihram for the Hajj from Mecca. Allah Almighty said: "Then whoever enjoys Umrah until the Hajj, then whatever offering is feasible." Abu Abdullah said: "A sheep, or more than that."

As for what one who intends the Hajj should do, Allah Almighty said: "Hajj is during well-known months, so whoever has made Hajj obligatory upon himself therein, let there be no lewdness nor wickedness nor quarreling in Hajj" [al-Baqarah: 197]. Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali said: The well-known months are Shawwal, Dhu al-Qidah, and Dhu al-Hijjah; Hajj is made obligatory in none but these. The making of Hajj obligatory upon oneself is the Talbiyah, the ishar, and the taqlid; whichever of these the one intending Hajj performs, he has made the Hajj obligatory upon himself. Lewdness is intercourse; wickedness is lying, cursing, and quarreling; and quarreling is a man's saying "No, by Allah" and "Yes, by Allah," and boasting.

Ali ibn al-Husayn (ع) said to Abu Khalid al-Wasiti, counseling him when he wished to perform Hajj: "O Abu Khalid, when you set out for Mecca, God willing, then hold to the fear of Allah, remember Him abundantly, and speak little except in good, for the completion of Hajj and Umrah lies in a man's guarding his tongue, as Allah Almighty said: No lewdness, no wickedness, no quarreling in Hajj. Lewdness is intercourse; wickedness is lying, cursing, and quarreling; and quarreling is a man's saying No, by Allah and Yes, by Allah, and boasting. Hold to scrupulousness, which restrains you from disobedience to Allah, to forbearance, by which you master your anger, and to good companionship with those who travel with you. There is no power except by Allah."

Abu Abdullah Jafar ibn Muhammad said to a man he was counseling: "When you go out as a pilgrim, hold to the fear of Allah, to forbearance, to scrupulousness, and to good companionship with those who accompany you, for Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali said: No account is taken of one who makes for this House if he does not possess three qualities: scrupulousness that restrains him from disobedience to Allah, forbearance by which he masters his anger, and good companionship for those who travel with him."

And Abu Abdullah Jafar ibn Muhammad said to a man who wished to perform Hajj, counseling him: "When you depart from your home intending Hajj or Umrah, make the supplication of relief and say: There is no god but Allah, the Forbearing, the Generous; there is no god but Allah, the Most High, the Great; glory be to Allah, Lord of the seven heavens and Lord of the seven earths and Lord of the Throne; and praise be to Allah, Lord of the worlds. O Allah, open before me in this journey of mine with good and seal it for me with good, and preserve me from every creature whose forelock You hold, for You, my Lord, are upon a straight path. In the name of Allah I have set out, in the name of Allah I enter, and for the sake of Allah I journey. O Allah, I place before me my forgetfulness and my haste. In the name of Allah and by the will of Allah, whatever of this journey of mine I remember or forget, O Allah, You are the One whose help is sought over all affairs, the Companion in travel and the Successor over family, wealth, and children. O Allah, make our journey easy for us, fold up the earth for us, and let us travel through it in obedience to You and obedience to Your Messenger. O Allah, set right our affairs for us, bless us in what You have provided us, and guard us from the torment of the Fire."

When you set out upon the road, there is a way to place yourself in Allah's keeping from the very first step. When you set your leg among the travelers, say: "In the name of Allah, and by Allah, and Allah is the Greatest." When you are settled in your journey, praise Allah seven times, glorify Him seven times, magnify Him seven times, and say "La ilaha ill Allah" seven times, and say: "Praise be to Allah who guided us to Islam and honored us with Muhammad and his family. Glory be to the One who has subjected this to us, for we could not have subdued it ourselves, and indeed to our Lord we shall return. Praise be to Allah, Lord of the worlds. O Allah, You are the Carrier upon the back and the Helper in affairs. O Allah, grant us the best of news, and lead us to Your forgiveness and Your pleasure. O Allah, no bird flies but by Your leave, and no good comes but from You, and none else protects. O Allah, I seek refuge in You from the hardships of travel, from the distress of turning back, and from an ill return to family, wealth, and children. O Allah, I ask You in this journey of mine for piety, for contentment, and for deeds that please You. O Allah, cut short the distance for me and make it easy, and set aright my affairs and leave me well among my family." And when you return, say in your returning: "We are those who repent, who worship, who praise, returning to our Lord, giving thanks, devoted, and to Him we return."

As for entering the city of the Prophet (ص), the Commander of the Believers (ع) said in his sermon that the Messenger of Allah (ص) declared Madinah sacred between the mountains of Ayr and Thawr, and that whoever causes an unlawful act therein or shelters one who commits it has upon him the curse of Allah, the angels, and all people, and Allah will not accept from him any obligatory or supererogatory act. Jafar ibn Muhammad said that the two sides of Madinah are sanctified. When he was asked what its two sides were, he answered that it is whatever the free woman surrounded with her garment, which is to say what the Messenger of Allah (ص) prohibited: disturbing its inhabitants, hunting its game, and cutting its trees.

It is recommended to perform a ritual bath before entering the city, and to begin the visit at the Mosque of the Prophet (ص). One should stand at his grave, offer prayers upon him and his family, and testify to his prophethood and his message. One should pray whatever one is able, and then pray two rakahs before sitting at the pillar that stands forward on the side of the grave, from the corner of the grave, for that is the place nearest the head of the Prophet (ص). Then one should rise, approach the grave, and say: "I bear witness that there is no god but Allah alone, with no partner; and I bear witness that Muhammad is His servant and messenger; and I testify that you are the Messenger of Allah, truly; and I testify that you are Muhammad ibn Abd Allah; and I testify that you conveyed the message of your Lord and gave sincere counsel to your nation, and that you strove in the way of Allah, and that you worshipped Allah until certainty came to you, and you fulfilled what was due upon you. This is the clear truth. May Allah reward you with the best of rewards. O Allah, bless Your servant and messenger Muhammad, as You have described him, as befits his faithfulness and the best of Your creation, and bless the family of Muhammad with the best, most complete, and most beautiful blessings, as You prayed upon one of Your prophets, Your purified ones, and the people of dignity, for You are Glorious and Generous. O Allah, bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad as You blessed Noah in the worlds; and bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad as You blessed Moses and Aaron; and bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad as You blessed Ibrahim and the family of Ibrahim, for You are the Praised, the Glorious. O Allah, whatever need I have of You, whether I have set about seeking it or have not, whether I have pronounced it or have not, whether I have known it in myself or have not, I ask You by the truth of Your messenger Muhammad and his blessed and noble grave, to graciously attend to all my needs, small and great."

He said that if one is able to remain in the city for three days, Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday, then let him pray on Wednesday between the grave and the pulpit, at the pillar that adjoins the grave, and let him strive there and ask Allah his needs for this world and the hereafter. Let him pray likewise and supplicate on Thursday at the pillar of repentance, and pray likewise and supplicate on Friday at the shrine of the Prophet (ص), opposite the large pillar, and let him fast those three days, for in that there is merit.

The Messenger of Allah (ص) said, as narrated from Ali: "Whoever visits my grave after my death is as one who migrated to me during my life. And whoever cannot visit my grave, let him convey peace upon me, for it will be reported to me." Jafar ibn Muhammad said that it is desirable for whoever witnesses the city to also visit the Mosque of Quba, which is the mosque founded upon piety, and al-Fadikh Mosque, and al-Fath Mosque, and the Mosque of Umm Ibrahim, and to pray there, and to visit the grave of Hamza (ع) and the graves of the martyrs, may Allah have mercy upon them. The last act of the pilgrim in Madinah should be to visit the grave of the Prophet (ص) and bid him farewell, saying at it what he said at the first.

The Messenger of Allah (ص) fixed the sites of ihram at five stations. For the people of Madinah and those allied with them it is al-Shajarah Mosque; for the people of Syria and those beyond them, al-Juhfah; for the people of Yemen, Yalamlam; for the people of al-Taif, Qarn; and for the people of Najd, al-Aqiq. Jafar ibn Muhammad said that these are the miqats of ihram for whoever wishes to perform Hajj or Umrah, or both together. To complete Hajj and Umrah, ihram must be assumed from the miqats the Messenger of Allah (ص) fixed. One must not pass them without entering ihram, nor enter ihram before reaching them. Whoever enters ihram before the miqat has departed from the Sunnah, and if he then spoils something of his ihram, nothing is required of him, because he was not truly in ihram, for one does not become a muhrim until he becomes one from the miqat. He said that whoever's home is closer to Mecca than the miqats should enter ihram from his home and is not obliged to travel to the miqat.

He also said, as part of the completion of Hajj and Umrah, that one should enter ihram in the vicinity of one's family. And he said that whoever fears the passing of the month for his Umrah may enter ihram before the miqat. So if a man departs in Rajab intending Umrah but knows he will not reach the miqat until Shaban, he must not leave off ihram until he reaches the miqat, and so his Umrah becomes a Shabani Umrah; but if he enters ihram in the remaining days of Rajab, before the miqat, his Umrah becomes a Rajabi Umrah, for the Rajabi is better, and that was what he intended.

He also said, concerning one who passed beyond the tree and did not enter ihram, that he should enter ihram from wherever lies between him and al-Juhfah. And he said that whoever comes to the miqat and forgets to enter ihram, or is ignorant of it, until he has passed it, or proceeds knowingly toward Mecca, is in a state that must be set right. If he is able to return to the miqat, let him return and enter ihram from it. If he fears missing the Hajj and cannot return, let him enter ihram from the place he has reached. And if he is already in Mecca, let him go out to the edge of the Haram and enter ihram from the unrestricted ground, then enter the Haram in a state of ihram; if he cannot, let him enter ihram from his place.

The Messenger of Allah (ص) commanded those who were with him during the Farewell Pilgrimage, when they reached al-Shajarah, to pluck the underarm hair, shave the pubic hair, and put off sewn garments, taking instead a waist-wrapper and a cloak. It is narrated from Abu Abd Allah Jafar ibn Muhammad that he said the muhrim should, along with that, use the tooth-stick, trim his nails, and take from his mustache, and that it does him no harm with which of these he begins. He should enter ihram directly after an obligatory or supererogatory prayer, and it is best for this to be after the sun has passed its zenith, though he is not confined to any hour, for ihram may be assumed by day or night, at any time when prayer is permitted. He said that the menstruating woman, upon reaching the miqat, should perform a ritual bath and enter ihram, and likewise the woman in post-natal bleeding. He said that the muhrim should not perfume himself before ihram with a scent whose fragrance will remain upon him after ihram. And if a man enters ihram without having prayed, nothing is upon him, though entering it after a prayer is better.

> أُحِلَّ لَكُمْ صَيْدُ ٱلْبَحْرِ وَطَعَامُهُۥ مَتَعًۭا لَّكُمْ وَلِلسَّيَّارَةِ

He said that when the muhrim wishes to enter ihram, he fixes his intention upon it and, following the prayer, utters what he binds himself to, whether Hajj, or Umrah, or Hajj alone, or Umrah alone. So he says: "O Allah, I intend to enjoy Umrah unto Hajj." Or he says: "O Allah, I intend to join Hajj to Umrah," if he has a sacrificial animal with him. Or he says: "O Allah, I intend Hajj," or "Umrah," whichever he wishes, and he mentions it upon Your Book and the Sunnah of Your Prophet: "O Allah, my place of release is wherever You detain me, by Your decree which You have decreed upon me. O Allah, help me in that and make it easy for me and accept it from me." Then he supplicates with whatever he wishes. And if he intends in his heart what he means to do, without pronouncing it, that suffices him and is valid.

He said that the best Hajj is tamattu, Umrah unto Hajj, which is the form upon which the Quran was revealed. When the Messenger of Allah (ص) performed the Farewell Hajj, he joined Hajj and Umrah because he had driven the sacrificial animal. When he reached Mecca, he circumambulated the House and performed say between Safa and Marwah, and Allah, Mighty and Majestic, revealed to him what He revealed, and he said: "Had I foreseen from my affair what I have now seen, I would not have driven the sacrificial animal, and I would have made it a tamattu." So whoever had no sacrificial animal with him was to release himself from ihram, and the people released themselves and made it an Umrah, except for those who had a sacrificial animal with them; then they entered ihram for Hajj from the Sacred Mosque on the Day of Tarwiyah. Thus enjoying Umrah unto Hajj is what the Messenger of Allah (ص) commanded, and this is as Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: it is for whoever's family is not present at the Sacred Mosque. As for those who are of the people of the Haram, it is not for them to enjoy Umrah unto Hajj, because they are able to perform Umrah whenever they wish. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, made this concession only for those who come from other lands, granting them that they perform Umrah and Hajj in a single journey, as a widening from Him and a kindness toward them.

> وَلَا تَحْلِقُوا۟ رُءُوسَكُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَبْلُغَ ٱلْهَدْىُ مَحِلَّهُۥ

Whoever enjoys Umrah unto Hajj, or performs Umrah on its own, enters ihram, then comes to Mecca and circumambulates the House seven circuits and prays the two rakahs of the tawaf, then walks between al-Safa and al-Marwah seven circuits, beginning with al-Safa and ending with al-Marwah, has thereby completed his Umrah. Let him then release himself from his ihram. If he is performing tamattu and intends the Hajj, he takes from the ends of his hair and his nails and remains disheveled, resembling the muhrim, until the Day of Tarwiyah, when he enters ihram for the Hajj from the Sacred Mosque, just as he entered ihram from the miqat. But if he is joining Hajj and Umrah and has driven the sacrificial animal, he does not release himself and remains in his ihram until he completes the Hajj, for Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says: ﴿وَلَا تَحْلِقُوا رُءُوسَكُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَبْلُغَ الْهَدْيُ مَحِلَّهُ﴾, "And do not shave your heads until the sacrificial animal reaches its place of slaughter" [al-Baqarah: 196]. As for whoever performs Hajj alone and comes to Mecca, there is no obligatory tawaf upon him until he returns from Arafah; and if he performs a voluntary tawaf, that is good and has merit in it. And if he comes to Mina without entering Mecca, there is no harm upon him.

As for the marking of the offering and the raising of the voice, Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali said that in former times people used to garland camels, cattle, and sheep, then they left off garlanding cattle and sheep. He said that to garland is to tie a strap or a cord upon what is driven for Hajj and Umrah, and this is done after entering ihram at the miqat, and one thus dedicates it. The gashing is that a cut is made in the right side of its hump with a blade until its blood flows, so that it is known to be an offering. And it is recommended to hang upon its garland a sandal in which its owner has prayed, so that if it strays it is known to be an offering. Jafar ibn Muhammad said that the offering is gashed on the right side of its hump, and it is covered and led along.

When one who has entered ihram from al-Shajarah reaches al-Bayda, he raises his voice with the talbiyah and the announcement, saying: "لَبَّيْكَ اللَّهُمَّ لَبَّيْكَ، لَبَّيْكَ لَا شَرِيكَ لَكَ لَبَّيْكَ، إِنَّ الْحَمْدَ وَالنِّعْمَةَ لَكَ وَالْمُلْكَ، لَا شَرِيكَ لَكَ," Labbayk Allahumma labbayk, labbayk la sharika laka labbayk, inna al-hamda wa al-nimata laka wa al-mulk, la sharika lak. He repeats the talbiyah likewise whenever he ascends a height or descends into a valley, whenever he meets a rider, at the end of every obligatory prayer, when he wakes from sleep, and at the hours before dawn, whether in a state of purity or not, though purity is better; and the more he says the talbiyah the better. It is recommended for a man to raise his voice with the talbiyah, while women lower their voices, saying it only so far as they hear themselves. Whoever needs to ride his offering out of necessity may ride it, and to drink of its milk when in need may drink, so long as this does not harm the animal. If it is an offering under a vow or an expiation, and something befalls it, whether through the owner's fault or otherwise, it is a guaranteed obligation, and he must replace it; but if it is a voluntary offering, nothing is upon him. And whatever is a guaranteed offering, he does not eat from it when he slaughters it, but gives it all in charity; whereas what is voluntary, he may eat from it, and feed others, and give in charity.

> يَٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ آمَنُوا۟ لَا تَقْتُلُوا۟ ٱلصَّيْدَ وَأَنتُمْ حُرُمٌۭ ۚ وَمَن قَتَلَهُۥ مِنكُم مُّتَعَمِّدًۭا فَجَزَآءٌۭ مِّثْلُ مَا قَتَلَ مِنَ ٱلنَّعَمِ

As for what is forbidden to the muhrim and what falls upon him if he does it, Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: ﴿الْحَجُّ أَشْهُرٌ مَعْلُومَاتٌ فَمَنْ فَرَضَ فِيهِنَّ الْحَجَّ فَلَا رَفَثَ وَلَا فُسُوقَ وَلَا جِدَالَ فِي الْحَجِّ﴾, "Hajj is in well-known months, and whoever has made the Hajj obligatory upon himself therein, there is no rafath and no transgression and no quarreling during the Hajj" [al-Baqarah: 197]. And it has already been said that the rafath is intercourse. He, Blessed and Exalted, said: ﴿وَلَا تَقْتُلُوا الصَّيْدَ وَأَنتُمْ حُرُمٌ﴾, "And do not kill game while you are in ihram" [al-Maidah: 95], and He whose remembrance is Majestic said: ﴿وَحُرِّمَ عَلَيْكُمْ صَيْدُ الْبَرِّ مَا دُمْتُمْ حُرُمًا﴾, "And forbidden to you is the game of the land as long as you are in ihram" [al-Maidah: 96]. And it has come from the People of the House (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) that the muhrim is barred from hunting, from intercourse, from perfume, and from wearing sewn garments.

The one who offers a sacrificial animal wounds it upon the right side of its hump with his iron blade until its blood flows, so that it may be known to be a sacrificial offering (هَدِي). It is recommended that he hang upon its collar a sandal in which its offerer has prayed, so that if it is found straying it is known to be a hadi. Jafar ibn Muhammad said: the sacrificial camels are marked on the right side of the hump, draped, and driven forward.

When the pilgrim reaches al-Bayda, having entered ihram from the tree, he raises the talbiyah and the ihlal, lifting his voice and saying: لَبَّيْكَ اللَّهُمَّ لَبَّيْكَ، لَبَّيْكَ لَا شَرِيكَ لَكَ لَبَّيْكَ، إِنَّ الْحَمْدَ وَالنِّعْمَةَ لَكَ وَالْمُلْكَ، لَا شَرِيكَ لَكَ. He renews the talbiyah in this way whenever he ascends a height, descends into a valley, or meets a rider, and at the close of every obligatory prayer, and when he wakes from sleep, and in the hours before dawn, whether he is in a state of purity or not. The more one calls the talbiyah, the better. It is recommended that a man raise his voice with it, and that women lower their voices, only so much that they may hear themselves.

Whoever needs to ride his sacrificial animal may ride it, and to drink of its milk, so long as this brings no harm to the animal. If the animal is harmed, whether through his use of it or otherwise, and it was pledged as a vow or as a compensation, it is a guaranteed offering, and he must furnish its replacement. If it was a voluntary offering, nothing is due from him. What is a guaranteed offering he does not eat from when he slaughters it, but gives it in charity; what was voluntary he may eat from, feed others with, and give in charity.

As to what is forbidden to the one in ihram and what he owes should he do it: Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said, ﴿الْحَجُّ أَشْهُرٌ مَعْلُومَاتٌ فَمَنْ فَرَضَ فِيهِنَّ الْحَجَّ فَلَا رَفَثَ وَلَا فُسُوقَ وَلَا جِدَالَ فِي الْحَجِّ﴾ [البقرة: ١٩٧], and it has already been said that the rafath is intercourse. He, Blessed and Exalted, said, ﴿وَلَا تَقْتُلُوا الصَّيْدَ وَأَنتُمْ حُرُمٌ﴾ [المائدة: ٩٥], and He, exalted be His mention, said, ﴿وَحُرِّمَ عَلَيْكُمْ صَيْدُ الْبَرِّ مَا دُمْتُمْ حُرُمًا﴾ [البقرة: ٩٦]. From the Ahl al-Bayt (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) it has come that the one in ihram is forbidden from hunting, from intercourse, from perfume, from wearing sewn clothing, from removing hair, and from trimming the nails, along with other matters we shall mention in this chapter and in the one that follows it, and what is owed for each, if Allah the Exalted wills.

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: whoever deliberately has intercourse with his wife while in ihram, knowing that this is not lawful for him, and reaches her, owes a sacrificial camel; and if he had not yet circled the House on the Day of Sacrifice, he must perform the Hajj again the following year. If the woman consented to him, knowing that this was not lawful for her, she too owes a sacrificial camel. If he compelled her, or came to her while she slept, nothing is due from her, and two camels are due from him. If both were ignorant that this was unlawful for them, nothing is due from either; but whichever of them knew must expiate and repeat the Hajj the following year. If he came to her other than through intercourse, he owes a camel and need not repeat the Hajj. If he did this in forgetfulness, nothing is due from him, and he is in the position of one who ate and drank in the daytime of Ramadan while forgetting.

He said: it is not lawful for the one in ihram to contract a marriage during his ihram, nor to conduct the marriage of another. If he does so, his marriage is void; and if he knew that this was unlawful, or the woman knew it, she is never afterward lawful for him. If both were ignorant, they are separated, and he may marry her once he leaves ihram, if he and she wish.

He said: if the one in ihram is intimate with his wife, or kisses her with desire, or deliberately looks at her with desire, or touches her with desire, and emits, he owes the blood of a sheep or more than that. But if this was without intent and without desire, and he emits, nothing is due from him.

He said: if the one in ihram disputes, saying "No, by Allah" and "Yes, by Allah," and is truthful in that, nothing is due from him, until he says it three times or more, whereupon he owes blood. But if he was lying, he owes blood for a single instance, and it increases as he adds to it.

He said: if the one in ihram shaves his head, he owes a sheep. If he removes some of the hair of his head or beard, he gives charity in proportion to that. If he merely wipes his head or beard and something slight falls from it, nothing is due from him. He said: if the one in ihram needs cupping, he may be cupped if he wishes; and if he shaves the place of the cupping-glasses, he gives a charity. If he clips all his nails, he owes blood; if he clips some of them, he gives charity in proportion to what he clipped.

The one in ihram was permitted, if he finds no sandals, to wear leather footwear cut below the ankles. He said: if the one in ihram fears for himself that he would be left uncovered, he may enter ihram in his clothing, and the recompense for that is the blood of a sheep, or charity, or fasting in proportion. The one in ihram may apply kohl if he needs it, but not with antimony, nor with kohl containing perfume; he may use the tooth-stick if he wishes, and may treat himself with anything lawful for him to eat, so long as it contains no perfume. It is disliked for the one in ihram to shade himself in the litter except from an ailment, and he is permitted this when he alights. If the one in ihram wears clothing, or does something forbidden to him, in ignorance or forgetfulness, nothing is due from him.

He said: there is no harm in entering ihram in a dyed garment, so long as it is not dyed with saffron, wars, or perfume; and white is better, and it is what is commanded, unless it cannot be found. He said: there is no harm in a woman in ihram wearing ornaments, so long as she does not display them to men. The one in ihram whom something harmful afflicts was permitted to remove it. He said: the man in ihram does not cover his head, nor the woman in ihram her face, though she may let a garment fall over her face without it clinging; and the man in ihram does not cover his ears. There is no harm, if he has a headache, in his binding his head, nor in his placing the waterskin upon it when he draws water. If he covers his head, or the woman in ihram covers her face, each of them gives a charity. There is no harm in the one in ihram washing, though it is disliked for him to plunge his head into the water.

As to the recompense for the game that the one in ihram takes: Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said, ﴿يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا لَا تَقْتُلُوا الصَّيْدَ وَأَنتُمْ حُرُمٌ وَمَن قَتَلَهُ مِنكُم مُتَعَمِّدًا فَجَزَاءٌ مِّثْلُ مَا قَتَلَ مِنَ النَّعَمِ﴾ [المائدة: ٩٥]. Thus did the Ahl al-Bayt recite it, "one possessing justice among you," in the singular, and he is the Imam (ع) and whoever the Imam appoints. There is a long argument concerning this, which we have abridged in this book for the sake of brevity.

Jafar ibn Muhammad said, concerning the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic, ﴿وَمَنْ عَادَ فَيَنتَقِمُ اللَّهُ مِنْهُ﴾ [المائدة: ٩٥]: whoever kills game while in ihram is judged to give its equivalent; and if he returns and kills another, he is not judged again, but Allah takes retribution from him, for the expiation is only for the first. He said: whoever takes game while in ihram owes its equivalent in livestock as an offering; if he is in Hajj, he slaughters the offering at Mina, and if he is in Umrah, then at Makkah. If he finds no animal, he gives its price in charity; and if he finds nothing to give in charity, he fasts. He said: if a sacrificial camel became due upon him and he could not find one, he feeds sixty poor persons; and if he finds nothing to feed with, he fasts eighteen days. Whoever owes a cow and cannot find one feeds thirty poor persons; and if he cannot, he fasts nine days. Whoever owes a sheep and cannot find one gives charity to ten poor persons; and if he cannot, he fasts three days. He said: whoever finds neither an offering nor the means to give charity should begin fasting; and if he then finds the means, he may continue his fast if he wishes, and it suffices him.

He said, concerning the one in ihram who takes an ostrich or a wild ass: for each of them a sacrificial camel; for the wild cow, a domestic cow; and for the gazelle, the dove, and their like among the birds, for each of these a sheep. If he takes the eggs of an ostrich, the stallion is set upon a number of she-camels equal to the eggs, and whatever they bring forth he offers, while for whatever is not brought forth nothing is due from him, for the eggs likewise include what does not come to term. If he breaks an egg and finds chicks within it, he must set the stallion upon the she-camels until they conceive and their burden is plain, and whatever they bring forth of that he offers, while for whatever miscarries nothing is due, for the chicks likewise include those that die before hatching. If he takes chicks that have quickened and in which the spirits have arisen, he sets the stallion upon a like number of she-camels until they conceive and their young stir in their wombs, and whatever they bring forth after that he offers, while for whatever miscarries or dies nothing is due from him, for the chicks in the egg, after the spirits have arisen in them, may still die. This principle concerning the eggs that the one in ihram takes has been transmitted only through the Imams of the Ahl al-Bayt.

For an Arab came to Umar (رض) in his day and said to him: O Commander of the Faithful, I set out for Hajj and entered ihram, then I passed by a nesting-place, that is, the place where the ostrich gathers its eggs until it secures them, and I took eggs there and roasted and ate them; what do you see for me in that? He said: Go to your companions, and whatever they judge for you I will confirm. He pointed to a group of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah (ص) who were seated in the mosque. The man went to them and asked, and they differed over him. He returned to Umar and said: There is a man here whom, when we differed over a matter, you would have decide it; send me to him. He said: And who is he? He said: Ali ibn Abi Talib. The man went to him and found him with al-Hasan and al-Husayn (عَلَيْهِمَا السَّلَامُ) before him, and told him his tale and asked him the answer. He said to him: Ask these two, gesturing toward al-Hasan and al-Husayn (عَلَيْهِمَا السَّلَامُ). The Arab said: What is this I meet from you, O Companions of Muhammad? Every time I ask one of you, he sends me on to another, until you have turned me back to the children. And he rose to depart. Some of those present said: These two are the sons of the Messenger of Allah (ص); ask them, O Arab, for with them you will find what you seek. So he asked them, and they said: You set a stallion of the camels upon young she-camels equal in number to the eggs you took, and whatever is brought forth of that you offer. The Arab said to Ali: Is it so? He said: Yes, O Arab. The man departed. Umar saw him and sent for him and asked him what had passed, and he told him. Umar said: Return to him and say that the young she-camels may miscarry, that is, cast their young before birth, or may not conceive. He returned and said this, and they said to him: And likewise the eggs may be addled, that is, spoil from their brooding so that no chicks come of them. He went back to Umar and told him, and Umar said: For this we were commanded to ask them. In another account, Umar asked them of this himself.

The explanation regarding the chicks that are struck, counted as living and dead, comes from Jafar ibn Muhammad (عَلَيْهِمَا السَّلَامُ) upon the same principle that came from Ali. He said, concerning the one in ihram who takes a wild cow, that he owes a domestic cow; and if he cannot find one, he feeds thirty poor persons; and if he cannot, he fasts nine days. And they said: concerning the fawn that the one in ihram takes, a sheep; and if he cannot provide a sheep, he feeds ten poor persons; and if he cannot, he fasts three days. The like binds everyone upon whom a sheep becomes due as a recompense for game, and we shall mention what is owed in that regard.

Whoever owes a recompense but can find neither the equivalent animal, nor the means to feed, nor the ability to fast, nothing further is due from him. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says, ﴿لَا يُكَلِّفُ اللَّهُ نَفْسًا إِلَّا وُسْعَهَا﴾ [البقرة: ٢٨٦], and He says, ﴿لَا يُكَلِّفُ اللَّهُ نَفْسًا إِلَّا مَا آتَاهَا﴾ [الطلاق: ٧]. Whoever has neither in his possession nor within his power what he is charged with owes nothing until he finds it or is able for it; and if the one upon whom this fell then attains something of what is due, he fulfills it, and if he becomes able to fast, he fasts, as we have said before.

They said, concerning the one in ihram who takes a hyena, a fox, or a hare, that a sheep is due for each one, or what stands in its place of feeding or fasting if he cannot provide the sheep, as we have described. They said that this was laid upon him as a penalty, that he might shun hunting. And they held that for the dove and its like, such as the wood-pigeon, the turtle-dove, the partridge, and whatever resembles them among the birds, there is due for each a sheep, or what stands in its place of feeding and fasting if it cannot be found. If he takes the egg of any of these, he does with the sheep as the one who takes the ostrich's eggs does with the camels, as has already been said.

They said, concerning the one in ihram who takes a monitor lizard, a jerboa, or the like, that a young kid is due for each. They said, concerning the one in ihram who takes game in the unconsecrated land, that the recompense is as already described; but if he takes it within the Sacred Precinct, both that recompense and the value of the game he took are due, the value given in charity, by way of severity and emphasis and magnifying of the Precinct, that he hold as trifling nothing of what Allah, Mighty and Majestic, and His Messenger (ص) have forbidden.

They said, concerning a single game animal taken by a company of people in ihram, that each of them severally bears the recompense, for each has transgressed in it. And they forbade the one in ihram to direct another to game, or to point it out, or to aid him in it. They permitted the one in ihram who is in dire need to eat of game, and it suffices him, and he does not eat carrion while he can find game.

They said: if the one in ihram casts at game and wounds it, or breaks its foreleg or hindleg, and it then flees and is lost to him so that he does not know what became of it, the full recompense is due from him. And if he takes it and it is sound and he releases it, there is due from him for the breaking of its foreleg or hindleg a quarter of the recompense, and for its wounding a portion of the recompense corresponding to the portion of the blood-money the wound would bear, given in charity.

If the one in ihram slaughters the game as his recompense, he does not eat of it, nor does he feed any other from it, neither one in the unconsecrated state nor one in ihram; and if he does, he must give the recompense a second time. They commanded that it be buried so that it not be eaten; and if it is dug up and eaten after he has buried it, nothing is due from him, so long as he did not order that or point it out. And this falls upon the one who took it.

They said, concerning the one in ihram who kills game by mistake or in ignorance, that the full recompense is due from him, as from one who did it deliberately; and this is well-nigh the consensus of the Muslims, most of them holding it. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, says, ﴿يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا لَا تَقْتُلُوا الصَّيْدَ وَأَنتُمْ حُرُمٌ وَمَن قَتَلَهُ مِنكُم مُتَعَمِّدًا فَجَزَاءٌ مِّثْلُ مَا قَتَلَ مِنَ النَّعَمِ﴾ [المائدة: ٩٥], and the Friends of Allah (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) know best the interpretation of His Book, Mighty and Majestic. Nothing in it, nor upon the tongue of the Messenger of Allah (ص), has made plain the lifting of the recompense from the one in ihram who kills game by mistake. As for the saying of the Messenger of Allah (ص), "Allah has lifted from my community their mistakes, their forgetfulness, and what they are compelled to," this does not enter into this meaning; he (ص) meant only that Allah has lifted from them the sin of that which they did not intend. For he (ص) made blood-money obligatory for mistaken killing.

The penalty owed for wounds inflicted upon a sane person stands upon its principle, and this same principle governs the one in ihram who kills game. The penalty is not waived for the person in ihram who accidentally kills game, for there is nothing in either the Book or the Sunnah to remove it from the one who committed it. Its basis is the words of Allah the Almighty: "O you who believe, Allah will test you with something of the game that your hands and your spears reach" [Al-Maidah: 94]. The verse addresses those in ihram, and whatever a person in ihram takes, whether by his hand or his weapon, falls within the matter Allah the Almighty has here set before them. What is established from the Imams (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) is binding because Allah the Almighty has joined obedience to them with obedience to Him and to His Messenger (ص); and what is established from the Messenger of Allah (ص) is a proof for whoever accepts it, as is the consensus of the Ummah. The full argument on this point is long, and what has been cited here suffices for one who consents to accept the truth, God willing.

They said that if a person in ihram catches a fish in the sacred precinct, he must release it; and likewise, if a person in ihram acquires game that was taken from the haram, he must return it to it. If a person in ihram kills small birds such as larks, sparrows, swifts, and their like, he owes a mudd of food for each one. For a locust, for whatever resembles or approaches it such as wasps and bees and the like, a handful of food is owed if it is killed deliberately. But whatever spreads upon him unbidden, or whatever his mount tramples without his intending it, carries nothing upon him. The same applies to the creeping things of the earth, such as beetles and their like, and likewise ants, corn weevils, flies, mosquitoes, ticks, and lice.

They permitted the person in ihram to kill whatever harms or endangers him: harmful flies, scorpions, snakes, birds of prey, and the four-footed vermin, the mouse and the biting dog, and whatever belongs to that same kind and runs its course and enters its category. There is nothing upon the person in ihram if he kills these. They permitted him to drive off the crow and the kite, and they made lawful for the person in ihram the catch of the sea, as Allah the Almighty has plainly permitted it in His Book, so that he may take it, eat of it, and provision himself from it. They said concerning the birds of the water and the marshes: whatever of them lays its eggs on land belongs to the game of the land, and whatever lays its eggs in the water belongs to the catch of the sea. They said that nothing counts among the game of birds except what takes independently to flight; as for domestic fowl and the like that dwell among buildings and houses, such as the Sindi hen and its kind, these are not game at all. And they said that whoever incurs a penalty for game slaughters it at Mina if he is a hajj pilgrim, or at Makkah if he is performing Umrah.

> أَوَلَمْ يَرَوْا۟ أَنَّا جَعَلْنَا حَرَمًا آمِنًۭا

As for the haram, its sanctity, its entry, and what is permitted within it: Allah the Almighty said, "Have they not seen that We have made a safe sanctuary?" [Al-Ankabut: 67]. It is reported from the Messenger of Allah (ص) that he forbade the game of the haram to be disturbed or its trees to be cut. On the day of the conquest of Makkah he (ص) stood with his back to the House, holding the doorpost, while the people of Makkah and others thronged before him, and he proclaimed: "There is no god but Allah, alone, with no partner. He fulfilled His promise, granted victory to His servant, and defeated the confederates alone. What do you say, O people of Makkah, and what do you expect?" They answered: "We say good, and we think well: a noble brother and the son of a noble brother, and you have power." He said: "I say as my brother Yusuf said: No blame upon you today; may Allah forgive you, and He is the most merciful of the merciful. Yet Allah the Almighty made Makkah sacred on the day He created the heavens and the earth, and it remains forbidden until the Day of Resurrection. Its game shall not be disturbed, its trees shall not be cut, its thorny shrubs shall not be uprooted, and its lost property shall not be taken except by one who will announce it." Al-Abbas said, "O Messenger of Allah, save for idhkhir, for it serves the graves and the houses." He meant that they would lay idhkhir over their dead in the graves as a guard against the earth and place it beneath them, and set it upon the roofs of their houses, and the goldsmiths would burn it as fuel. So the Messenger of Allah (ص) said, "Save for idhkhir." He spoke much beside this at that place, and by these words the sanctity of the haram is defined and preserved.

He (ص) commanded that none of the haram's trees be struck down and that its game not be scared off in defiance of its sanctity. He permitted from it only what is truly needed, such as a staff for the shepherd or what makes his bucket serviceable, along with fruits, and what enters upon people in their dwellings among the trees, and what the beasts and camels eat and graze with their mouths, and what people plant themselves, as against what the haram itself brings forth. Whoever takes anything of that must give in charity its value.

It is recommended that the one entering the haram perform a ritual bath before he enters it. The one joining Umrah to Hajj cuts off the talbiyah when he beholds the houses of Makkah, and after that he calls out the takbir and the tahlil. In Hajj the talbiyah is cut off when the sun declines on the Day of Arafah, and in the separate Umrah when he enters the haram. When the hajj pilgrim, the umrah performer, the qarin, or the mutamatti enters Makkah, he first secures his baggage and safeguards his belongings if he has a mount and gear, then goes out before occupying himself with anything and begins with the Sacred Mosque. He comes to it with tranquility and dignity, holding in his heart the exaltation of the House. Best for him is to walk to it barefoot, though there is no harm if he wears sandals or rides. It is recommended that he enter by the Gate of Bani Shayba, which is the Gate of the Iraqis, and that he supplicate with whatever he is able and finds ready.

Among what has come as supplication at that moment, from Jafar ibn Muhammad (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ), he commanded: "When you enter Makkah and set down your baggage, begin with the Sacred Mosque; and when you reach it, stand at the gate facing the Stone and say: In the name of Allah, and by Allah, and to Allah, and as Allah wills, and upon the creed of the Messenger of Allah (ص). The finest of names belong to Allah, and praise be to Allah, and peace be upon the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon Muhammad ibn Abdillah, peace be upon you, O Prophet, and the mercy of Allah and His blessings. Peace be upon the prophets of Allah and His messengers. Peace be upon Ibrahim and Ismail. Peace be upon the messengers and upon the righteous servants of Allah. O Allah, send blessings upon Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, and have mercy upon Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, and bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, as You blessed and had mercy upon and blessed Ibrahim and the family of Ibrahim among the worlds; surely You are Praiseworthy, Glorious. O Allah, send blessings upon Muhammad, Your servant and Your Messenger, and send blessings upon Ibrahim, Your intimate friend, and upon Your prophets and messengers. Open for me the gates of Your mercy, employ me in Your obedience and in what pleases You, and preserve me with the safekeeping of faith so long as You keep me alive. Exalted be the praise of Your Face. Praise be to Allah who has made me among His delegation and among the visitors of His House. Upon every place that is come to and every one who is visited there is a due for whoever comes and visits; and You, O Allah, are the best who is come to and the best who is visited. So I ask You by Allah, O Most Merciful, by that You are Allah, there is no god but You alone, with no partner, and that Muhammad is Your servant and Messenger, and that You are One, Eternal, who did not beget and was not begotten, and to whom none is equal: O Bountiful, O Majestic, O Tender, O Generous, make Your gift to me, and the first thing You grant me, my release from the Fire. O Allah, free my neck from the Fire." He says this three times, then: "And widen for me lawful, wholesome provision, and ward off from me the evil of the devils of jinn and men, and the evil of the corrupt among Arab and non-Arab."

Then draw near the House, raise your hands, and say: "O Allah, I ask You, in this my standing, at the beginning of my rites, that You accept my repentance, that You pass over my offense, and that You lift from me my burden. Praise be to Allah who has brought me to His Sacred House." Then advance to the corner and kiss the Black Stone if you are able and it is within your reach, or touch it, which is to lay your hands upon it; and if you cannot come to that, point toward it with your hand. Then take up the tawaf.

> ثُمَّ لْيَقْضُوا۟ تَفَثَهُمْ وَلْيُوفُوا۟ نُذُورَهُمْ وَلْيَطَّوَّفُوا۟ بِٱلْبَيْتِ ٱلْعَتِيقِ

Concerning the tawaf, Allah the Almighty said, "And let them circumambulate the Ancient House" [Al-Hajj: 29], and He, Blessed and Exalted, said, "Purify My House for those who circumambulate, those who abide there, and those who bow and prostrate" [Al-Baqarah: 125]. Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ) was asked about the origin of the tawaf, and he said that when Allah the Almighty created Adam, He commanded the angels to prostrate to him and favored him with the names He taught him, and they held themselves great in their souls and said, "We did not think that Allah the Almighty would create a creature more honored in His sight than we." And when He told them He would settle him upon the earth, they said, "Will You place therein one who will spread corruption in it and shed blood?" and so they objected to Allah the Almighty in this. He, Blessed and Exalted, said to them, "Did I not tell you that I know the unseen of the heavens and the earth, and I know what you reveal," meaning this speech they uttered, "and what you were concealing" [Al-Baqarah: 33], meaning what they had said within themselves and held great concerning the affair of Adam. They came to know that they had fallen into offense, so they took refuge at the Throne and circled it seven times, seeking their Lord's pleasure, and He was pleased with them and said, "Descend and build for Me a house upon the earth, that whoever of My servants disobeys Me may circle it, so that I may be pleased with them as I have been pleased with you." So they built a house at the place of the House. The first to circle it was Adam, and so it remained until it was raised up in the time of the Flood. Then Allah the Almighty revealed to Ibrahim, and he built the House upon its foundation.

Abbad ibn Bakr al-Basri said to Jafar ibn Muhammad (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ), "O son of the Messenger of Allah, we have received a report from the Messenger of Allah (ص) that we wish to lay before you: if it is true in your sight we will accept it, and if it is false we will leave it." He said, "What is it?" He said, "It has reached us that he (ص) said: Whoever rises after the first part of the night has passed, completes his ablution, touches the Stone, testifies that there is no god but Allah and that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah, then circles seven times and prays two rakahs in which he recites, after the Opening of the Book, Say, O disbelievers and Say, He is Allah, One, in whichever part of the mosque he wishes, then stands between the Stone and the door and praises Allah the Almighty as He deserves and sends blessings upon me and upon the people of my House, his feet shall not stir until he is granted his request, eight gates of Paradise are opened for him to enter by whichever he wishes, and Allah the Almighty writes for him three thousand good deeds, effaces from him three thousand evil deeds, and raises him three thousand degrees." Abu Abdillah (عَلَيْهِ السَّلَامُ) said, "All of that is true and sound, and it is six thousand."

He said: When the one performing tawaf begins at the House and kisses the Black Stone, or touches it, or points to it, he takes up the circling, going to his right and keeping the House upon his left, and circles it seven times, beginning at the Black Corner and ending at it. When he sets out from it and circles the House until he returns to it, that is one circuit, reckoned as such, and so on until he completes seven circuits, moving at a brisk pace for three and walking for four. The brisk pace is a swift manner of walking, and thus did the Messenger of Allah (ص) do, and he commanded his companions to do so, and they did. For it had reached him that Quraysh said the companions of Muhammad had grown weak and the fever of Yathrib had worn them down, so he wished to show them the hardiness and strength of his companions. Yet if the one performing tawaf leaves off the brisk pace in these three circuits, or in some of them, there is nothing upon him; and there is no brisk pace upon women.

In every tawaf of the House, the one performing it kisses the Black Corner and the Yemeni Corner, or touches them if he is able, or points to them if he cannot. He circles from behind the Hijr and does not enter the Hijr; if out of ignorance he enters it, he comes out and resumes his tawaf from behind it. He does not touch the two corners that adjoin the Hijr. Whoever is unable to circle walking is carried and circled while borne; circling barefoot is better, though there is no harm in circling in sandals. Reciting and supplication are recommended during the tawaf, and there is no harm in permitted speech during it.

Among what has been narrated from Jafar ibn Muhammad (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ) as supplication in the tawaf, he commanded that when you circle the House and come opposite the waterspout, you say, "O Allah, free me from the Fire"; and when you come opposite the Black Stone, you say, "O Allah, grant me good in this world and good in the Hereafter, and guard me from the torment of the Fire." And when you are in the seventh of your circuits and reach the Stone, you cling to the House, pressing your belly and face against it, and say: "Praise be to Allah who has honored you, ennobled you, and magnified you, and made you a place of return for people and a sanctuary. O Allah, the House is Your House and the servant is Your servant, and this is the standing-place of one who seeks refuge with You from the Fire. O Allah, I am poor before You, fearful of You, and seeking Your protection. Do not alter my body and do not change my name. O Allah, I am weak, so strengthen my weakness in Your good pleasure, and multiply my deed and accept it from me. O Allah, grant me good in this world and good in the Hereafter, and guard us from the torment of the Fire." He said that there are angels there who say "Amen" to the supplication of the believer.

The one performing tawaf should not circle except while pure and in a state of ablution. If he performs the obligatory tawaf without ablution, he repeats the tawaf; and if he performs a voluntary tawaf without ablution, he makes ablution and prays the two rakahs of tawaf, and there is nothing upon him. If he breaks his ablution or his nose bleeds during the tawaf, he makes ablution, or washes his nose of the blood, and then builds upon his tawaf if it is voluntary; and if it is obligatory and he has passed the half or more, he builds upon his tawaf, but if it is the half or less, he begins the tawaf anew.

The obligatory tawaf for the mutamatti and the qarin is the tawaf of Umrah, performed first when he enters Makkah, then the tawaf of Hajj when he returns from Mina, which is also the tawaf of ifadah. With each of these there is a say between al-Safa and al-Marwah, whose manner we shall describe. Then, after the tawaf of ifadah and its say, comes the tawaf of the women, which is a circling of the House alone. This is the obligatory tawaf, each tawaf of it being seven circuits. The tawaf of farewell upon departing from Makkah is recommended and encouraged, and beyond these there is voluntary tawaf, in which there is merit: whoever wishes may circle as much as he wishes, by night or by day, save that he circle no fewer than seven at a time.

He said that there are angels who say "Amen" to the prayer of the believer.

One should never perform tawaf except in a state of ritual purity, having made ablution. If a person performs the obligatory tawaf without ablution, he must repeat the circumambulation. If he circumambulates voluntarily without ablution, then there are no two rakahs of tawaf for him to pray and nothing further is required of him. Should he experience a discharge or break his ablution during the tawaf, he performs ablution and washes the affected place, then continues. If the tawaf was voluntary, he builds upon what he has already completed, even if the interruption came at the very beginning. If it was the obligatory tawaf, and he had already passed the halfway point, he builds upon it; but if he had completed only half or less, he begins the tawaf anew.

The obligatory tawaf is of several kinds. There is the tawaf of Umrah, performed upon first entering Mecca; then the tawaf of Hajj, performed when one returns from Mina, which is the tawaf al-Ifadah. Each of these is joined by a sai between al-Safa and al-Marwah, which we shall describe in its proper place. After the tawaf al-Ifadah and its sai comes the tawaf of women, a circumambulation of the house on its own. This is the obligatory circumambulation, and each part of it consists of seven rounds. The farewell tawaf, performed upon departing from Mecca, is highly recommended and much encouraged. Beyond these, all other circumambulation is voluntary and carries great reward; whoever wishes may circumambulate as much as he wishes by night or by day, provided he does not perform less than a full week of seven rounds.

He said: The menstruating woman and the woman in a state of lochia complete all the rites once they have washed, cleansed, and purified themselves, except for the tawaf; this she does not perform until she is pure. The woman with irregular bleeding may circumambulate and perform all the rites, so long as she is not in a confirmed state of menstruation. The menstruating woman may perform the sai between al-Safa and al-Marwah once she is purified, and she makes up the tawaf and the sai afterward. Women should hasten their tawaf and their visit if they fear the onset of menstruation.

The Imams granted a concession to anyone wearied during the tawaf or the sai: he may rest and then build upon the tawaf and sai he has already completed. If a prescribed prayer is called while a person is in the midst of tawaf, he interrupts his circumambulation at the point he has reached, prays, and then resumes, building upon what has passed. He should not perform the obligatory tawaf hastily and without reason, nor should he begin it when he judges that a prayer time is about to enter, or while the imam is praying and he has not yet completed his tawaf and sai. Rather, he delays the tawaf until he has prayed the obligatory prayer, and then he circumambulates.

The Imams also permitted the interruption of a voluntary tawaf for some occasion of righteousness that presents itself. They narrated of al-Husayn ibn Ali that he was circumambulating the house voluntarily when a man came to him with a need. He broke off his tawaf and went with the man. When he was questioned about this, he said: How could I not go with him, when the Messenger of Allah (صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ) said, "Whoever walks with his believing brother in his need, even if the need is not fulfilled, has an Umrah recorded for him for accompanying him; and if the need is fulfilled, a Hajj and an Umrah are recorded for him." I went with this man in his need, and it was fulfilled, so I gained a Hajj and an Umrah, and then I returned to my tawaf.

They said: Whoever is overcome by drowsiness during the obligatory tawaf or its sai and cannot complete it builds upon what he has done if he has passed the halfway point, and begins again if he has not. If he sets it aside until he is rested and then completes it himself, there is nothing against him. If doubt arises during the tawaf, he builds upon what is certain. And if a person adds to his rounds, performing eight, he joins to that eighth round six more so that he has completed two full weeks; the first week is his obligatory tawaf and the second is voluntary. He then prays four rakahs, two for each week, and performs the sai between al-Safa and al-Marwah for seven rounds. If he wishes, he may perform another seven voluntarily, and if he wishes, he may leave them. One should not deliberately join two weeks together without separating them by two rakahs, except in the case of forgetfulness we have described.

When the pilgrim has completed a week of tawaf around the house, he draws back to the Maqam of Ibrahim, placing it between himself and the house, and prays two rakahs. For Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has said, "And take from the Maqam of Ibrahim a place of prayer" [Al-Baqarah: 125]. No one should pray these rakahs except from behind the Maqam, keeping it between himself and the house.

Whoever performs the obligatory tawaf after the Asr prayer prays its two rakahs at that time. But if his tawaf was voluntary, he delays the two rakahs until the disliked hour has passed; likewise, if he performs a voluntary tawaf after the Fajr prayer, he does not pray its two rakahs until the time when voluntary prayer becomes permissible, as we mentioned in the chapters on prayer. If, however, the tawaf was obligatory, he prays its two rakahs the moment he finishes, at whatever hour that may be. It is recommended that in the two rakahs of tawaf, after the opening of the Book, he recite Surat al-Kafirun in the first rakah and Surat al-Ikhlas in the second. All of this is the teaching of the Ahl al-Bayt (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِمْ).

They said: After completing and praying the two rakahs of tawaf, he returns to the Black Stone and touches it in reverence, then goes to Zamzam. He drinks of its water and pours some over himself if he is able, then goes out through the gate of al-Safa. He praises Allah, declares His oneness and His greatness, and supplicates with whatever comes readily to him, while he faces the house in the direction of the Black Corner.
