It is related from Jafar ibn Muhammad (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ), traced back to the Messenger of Allah (صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ), that upon al-Safa during the Farewell Pilgrimage he said: "There is no god but Allah alone, who has no partner. To Him belongs the dominion and to Him belongs all praise. He gives life and causes death, while He Himself is ever-living and does not die. In His hand is all good, and He is capable of all things. There is no god but Allah alone. He has fulfilled His promise, aided His servant, and defeated the confederates alone." He said this three times. And Abu Abdillah would say after it: "O Allah, I ask You for pardon and well-being in this world and the hereafter. I ask You, O Lord, to send blessings upon Muhammad and his pure household, to strengthen me in acting upon what You have taught me of Your right, and to spread over me some of Your provision. I ask You, O Lord, for good in this world and good in the hereafter, and I ask You to shield me from the torment of the Fire."

He said: When you have finished your supplication upon al-Safa, descend and walk at a measured, tranquil pace. When you reach the bottom of the valley at the point marked by the first beacon, which is the flag marking the start of the striving, quicken your pace until you have risen from the belly of the valley and reached the second beacon, likewise a flag marking the end of the striving. It is for this that this rite is called the sai between al-Safa and al-Marwah. The first to perform the sai was Ibrahim. When he descended from al-Safa and reached the belly of the valley, Iblis, may Allah curse him, blocked his way, and Ibrahim hastened as if fleeing from him until he took hold of the far edge of the valley on the other side, whereupon the accursed one turned away from him, and he walked on.

He said: Among what is recommended to say during the sai is: "In the name of Allah; Allah is the greatest; and may Allah send blessings upon Muhammad and his family. O Allah, forgive, have mercy, and overlook what You know, for You are the Most Mighty, the Most Generous." When he leaves the place of quickened pace and comes to where he walks at his ordinary pace, he raises his hands toward the heavens and says: "O Lord of grace, bounty, favor, blessings, and generosity, my deed is weak, so multiply it for me and accept it from me, and send blessings upon Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, as You are worthy of and as they are worthy of from You."

He said: There is no quickened pace upon women; they walk throughout. The quickened pace is for the men. He then ascends al-Marwah and says what he said upon al-Safa, returns to al-Safa and stands upon it likewise, and returns again to al-Marwah, until he has completed seven rounds, beginning with al-Safa and ending with al-Marwah, striving in each round and supplicating as described above. If he begins with al-Marwah before al-Safa, he discards that round and begins the sai afresh, starting with al-Safa and ending with al-Marwah. For when the Messenger of Allah (صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ) ascended al-Safa, he recited the verse, "Indeed al-Safa and al-Marwah are among the rites of Allah" [Al-Baqarah: 158], and then said, "Begin with that which Allah began with." This is how whoever circumambulates the house should perform the sai, joining the sai to the tawaf, as the Messenger of Allah (صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ) did. If he separates the two for a valid reason, there is no harm, as when he is wearied and rests, or is pressed for time, or is presented with something he cannot decline. But he should not delay it without excuse.

> وَأَنزَلْنَآ إِلَيْكَ ٱلذِّكْرَ لِتُبَيِّنَ لِلنَّاسِ مَا نُزِّلَ إِلَيْهِمْ

Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ) was asked whether the sai between al-Safa and al-Marwah is obligatory, and he said, "Yes." He was asked whether the shortening of prayer while traveling is likewise obligatory, and he said, "Yes." It was said to him: "But Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said, And when you journey through the land, there is no blame upon you if you shorten the prayer, if you fear that those who disbelieve may afflict you [al-Nisa: 101], and He said of Hajj and Umrah that there is no blame upon one who walks between them." He answered that Allah, Mighty and Majestic, made mention of this in His Book, and the Messenger of Allah (صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ) made it clear by his word and his deed and his command. He is the one whom Allah charged with the clarification, for He said, "And We have revealed to you the Reminder, that you may make clear to the people what has been sent down to them" [al-Nahl: 44], and He made it obligatory upon the people to accept from him, for He said, "And whatever the Messenger gives you, take it, and whatever he forbids you, abstain from it" [al-Hashr: 7].

In the saying of Allah, "Indeed al-Safa and al-Marwah are among the rites of Allah, so whoever makes Hajj to the house or performs Umrah, there is no blame upon him for walking between them" [al-Baqarah: 158], there is proof that the sai between them is obligatory. For had its omission been a matter left to choice, He would have said, "there is no blame upon him if he does not walk between them." Rather, the words "there is no blame upon him for walking between them" came only after some had held that there was blame in it, so Allah made clear that it is among His rites which He has ordained, and that there is no sin upon one who performs it. For it is said that the Ansar used to enter the state of consecration for the idol Manat, which stood level with Qudayd, and so they were reluctant to walk between al-Safa and al-Marwah. Allah informed them that there was no blame upon them in it, and that the two are among His rites. In the same way, when the Messenger of Allah (صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ) commanded the shortening of prayer and the breaking of the fast while traveling, some of the Muslims found it weighty and burdensome to do, and Allah informed them that there was no blame upon them in it. Jafar ibn Muhammad (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ) said: Whoever deliberately abandons the sai must perform the Hajj again in the following year; and whoever forgets it appoints someone to make it up on his behalf, just as one who forgets the two rakahs of tawaf appoints someone to make them up.

Concerning the one who joins Umrah to Hajj in the manner of tamattu:

> وَلَا تَحْلِقُوا۟ رُءُوسَكُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَبْلُغَ ٱلْهَدْىُ مَحِلَّهُۥ

Jafar ibn Muhammad (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ) spoke of the one who performs tamattu, joining Umrah to Hajj, meaning whoever brings the two together in a single journey, enters ihram for both at once, and consecrates himself for them together, as we mentioned in the chapter on ihram. He said: When the one performing tamattu has circumambulated the house and made the sai between al-Safa and al-Marwah, intending by them the tawaf and sai of the Umrah, he has fulfilled it. He should then release himself from ihram, taking something from his hair and his nails, and keeping the rest for the Hajj. So too the Messenger of Allah (صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ) commanded in the Farewell Pilgrimage: those with him who had not driven a sacrificial offering released themselves from ihram once they had performed the tawaf and sai and became fully unbound. But he himself (صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ), having driven the offering, did not release himself, and he joined the Umrah to the Hajj, in keeping with the word of Allah, "And do not shave your heads until the offering reaches its place of sacrifice" [al-Baqarah: 196], meaning until the offering is slaughtered. It was at that time that Allah sent down the command of tamattu, and the Prophet said, "Had I foreseen from the start of my affair what I now perceive at its end, I would not have driven the offering, and I would have made it a tamattu." He urged the people toward it, for they had not known this; they knew Umrah only as a rite performed on its own.

> وَلَا تَحْلِقُوا۟ رُءُوسَكُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَبْلُغَ ٱلْهَدْىُ مَحِلَّهُۥ

The Commander of the Believers, Ali ibn Abi Talib (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ), arrived that day from Yemen, having driven an offering. He found Fatimah (عَلَيْهَا السَّلَامُ), who was among those performing Hajj with the Messenger of Allah (صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ), wearing dyed garments and released from her ihram, and this was held against her. But the Messenger of Allah (صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ) had commanded her to do so. He came to the Prophet and informed him of it, and the Prophet said, "Yes." Then he said, "And you, O Ali, for what did you enter ihram?" Ali said, "I said: O Allah, I enter ihram for that which Your Messenger has entered ihram." The Prophet said, "Then do not release yourself until you have sacrificed the offering; perform the tawaf and the sai for your Umrah, and remain as you are in your ihram until you are released from the Hajj." And Ali shared with him (صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ) in his offering; what he had brought from Yemen was combined with what the Messenger of Allah had driven, and he was made a partner in the whole of it.

Abu Abdillah Jafar ibn Muhammad (صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ) was asked how the one performing tamattu should deal with the shaving of the head. He said: He does not shave his head with the razor, but shortens it or takes from its edges, keeping some for his Hajj. It was asked: And if he does shave? He said: If he shaves and his hair has not grown back between then and the day of sacrifice, he owes a blood offering, that is, a sheep which he slaughters and whose meat he gives in charity. He said: The razor is passed over his head on the day of sacrifice whether the hair has grown back or not; and likewise the bald man, who has no hair upon his head, passes the razor over it in place of shaving.

The pilgrim who performs tamattu owes his attention first to the ordering of his release. If he forgets to shorten his hair until he has already entered ihram for Hajj, and only then remembers, he should seek Allah's forgiveness, and nothing further is required of him. Once he has been released from his tamattu, he may travel between that release and his entry into ihram for Hajj as he wishes; but he should not perform any voluntary tawaf after the tawaf of tamattu until he has dissolved his ihram and shortened his hair.

When he has completed his Umrah, he should neither wear the ihram garments again nor make himself resemble those still in ihram. The people of Makkah, likewise, should hold themselves in a manner befitting the days of Hajj, and if they do not perform Hajj they remain in their ordinary state. If the one in ihram comes to his wife before he has shortened his hair, he owes a camel; if he kisses her, he owes a sheep; and once he has shortened, he may come to her if he wishes and nothing is upon him, for what was forbidden to him during his ihram has become lawful upon his release from it. If the one performing tamattu arrives in Makkah on the day of Tarwiyah, he performs the tawaf of his Umrah and the sai, walks between the two hills, and then enters ihram for Hajj on that same day.

As for the woman: permission is given to her, if she fears that menstruation will keep her from the tawaf and sai of Hajj, to perform them before she sets out for Hajj. And if, having joined Umrah to Hajj in tamattu, she menstruates before she can perform the tawaf of Umrah, she delays it until she becomes pure, and then performs it before the tawaf of Hajj.

He said: there is no tamattu for the people of Makkah. Allah Almighty permitted it only for those who come from distant lands, and this is the meaning of His saying: ﴿ذَلِكَ لِمَنْ لَمْ يَكُنْ أَهْلُهُ حَاضِرِي الْمَسْجِدِ الْحَرَامِ﴾ "That is for one whose family is not present at the Sacred Mosque." [al-Baqarah: 196]. This is because the people of Makkah may perform Umrah whenever they wish. And he said: whoever enters the Sacred Precinct and performs Umrah in one of the months of Hajj, then remains there until he performs Hajj, that man has performed tamattu.

> وَلَا تَحْلِقُوا۟ رُءُوسَكُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَبْلُغَ ٱلْهَدْىُ مَحِلَّهُۥ

The one who performs tamattu, joining Umrah to Hajj, owes what Allah Almighty named: ﴿فَمَا اسْتَيْسَرَ مِنَ الْهَدْيِ﴾ "then whatever offering can be readily obtained." [al-Baqarah: 196], which is a sheep or something greater. He slaughters it during the days of Mina and distributes its meat in charity, eating none of it himself. If he cannot find a sheep, he takes a share in a camel, and the required portion of the camel will be explained in the section on the hady. If he can find neither, then he fasts, as Allah Almighty commanded, three days during the Hajj: one day before Tarwiyah, the day of Tarwiyah, and the day of Arafah; and seven days once he has returned to his family. If he fasts those seven on the road home, that suffices him. If he fasted neither the three days during the Hajj nor on the way back, whether out of ignorance or forgetfulness, then he lets them go and fasts ten days upon returning to his family. To fast them continuously is better, but if he separates them, that too suffices.

When the day of Tarwiyah comes, the one performing tamattu enters ihram for Hajj from the Sacred Mosque, just as a pilgrim enters ihram from the miqat. The people of Makkah enter ihram from their own houses rather than from the miqat outside the city; and whoever resides in Makkah without being of its people enters ihram, likewise, from the place where he resides.

### Going out to Mina and the standing at Arafah

Jafar ibn Muhammad said to one of those whom he was teaching the rites, among those performing tamattu: on the day of Tarwiyah, Allah willing, bathe, put on your garments, and enter the Sacred Mosque barefoot, with serenity and dignity. Perform, if you wish, seven rounds of voluntary tawaf upon your arrival, and pray the two rakahs of your tawaf. When the sun has passed its zenith, pray the obligatory prayer, then enter ihram for Hajj and do just as you did at Dhu al-Hulayfah. Then go out to Mina.

He said: the people should leave Makkah for Mina on the day of Tarwiyah, from the beginning of the day until its end. It was named the day of Tarwiyah because the people would water their animals on it, for there was no water in Mina at that time. It is the eighth day of Dhu al-Hijjah. When the people went out from Makkah toward Mina and Arafah, intending the Hajj, whoever wished among them rode and whoever wished walked; and for one who could find no mount, there is reward in walking.

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: for one who is able, riding is better than walking, because the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and his family) went out riding. Jabir ibn Abdullah (رض) said: "I looked out that day upon the company before the Messenger of Allah, people on foot and people mounted, as far as the eye could reach, and on his right and his left and behind him the like of that, between rider and walker." When they reached Kura al-Ghamim, the pedestrians drew themselves up in a line, seeking the blessing of his supplication. As he passed by them they said, "O Messenger of Allah, the journey has grown long upon us and the walking has become hard for us." He said, "May Allah reward you," and then said, "If you were to help yourselves with a light quick pace, your bodies would grow lighter and you would cover the ground the sooner." They did so, and it eased them. So the rider is stronger for the standing at Arafah and for supplication than the man on foot, and for that reason riding is recommended for the one who finds a mount and is able.

He said: when the season is upon them, the imam should not pray the noon prayer on the day of Tarwiyah except at Mina, and he passes the night there, and the people pass the night with him. Then he goes out, and the people go out with him, from Mina to Arafah on the morning of the day of Arafah, after the sun has risen. He said: it is recommended to bathe on that day, and to break one's fast, for anyone who fears that fasting would weaken him for the standing and the supplication at Arafah. When they come to Arafah they descend there, and it is recommended to descend and to stand near the mountain. To descend and stand at the place of al-Idrak is forbidden; Jafar ibn Muhammad said that those who descend beneath it and stand there have no valid Hajj. All the rest of Arafah is a valid standing-place.

He said: when the sun passes its zenith, the assembly gathers. The imam comes to the floor of the valley and addresses the people; then the muezzin calls the adhan and gives the iqamah, and the imam leads them in the noon prayer. Then the muezzins give the iqamah again, and he leads them in the afternoon prayer, the people praying with him, combining the two prayers with a single adhan and two iqamahs. So did the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and his family). Then he stands, and the people stand with him, and they remain standing, their faces turned toward the sun, calling upon Allah Almighty, yearning toward Him, and asking Him for their needs of this world and the next, until the sun sets. Whoever wishes recites the Quran, and whoever wishes glorifies Allah, declares His oneness, exalts Him, and seeks His forgiveness.

It is narrated of Muhammad ibn Ali ibn al-Husayn that when he stood at Arafah he would begin Surah al-Baqarah, and after every ten verses he would glorify Allah, declare His oneness, exalt Him, seek forgiveness, and supplicate; then he would begin the next ten and do likewise, until he had completed the surah.

It is narrated of Jafar ibn Muhammad that he said: when you stand at the standing-place of Arafah, praise Allah and extol Him, and pray upon the Prophet Muhammad and his family, then say:

"O Allah, I ask You by Your might, by Your strength, by Your generosity, by Your bounty, by Your grace, and by all that comes from You, O most Hearing of those who hear, most Seeing of those who see, O Swift in reckoning, O Ruler of rulers, O Most Merciful of the merciful, that You send blessings upon Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, and that You do with me what is worthy of You." Then raise your hand and your face toward the sky and say: "O Allah, my need is such that if You grant it nothing You withhold will harm me, and if You withhold it nothing You grant will profit me. I ask You for the deliverance of my neck from the Fire. O Allah, I am Your servant, my forelock is in Your hand, and my term is within Your knowledge. I ask You to grant me success in what pleases You with me, and to make me one whose deed You are pleased with and whose striving You accept." Then say: "Praise be to Allah for His forbearance after His knowledge; praise be to Allah for His pardon after His power; praise be to Allah for His mercy that outstrips His wrath; and praise be to Allah with every praise that is His, for all His favors," until you conclude, "praise be to Allah in the measure that our Lord loves and is pleased with; praise be to Allah for His favors, which can neither be numbered nor ever repaid. O Allah, I ask You by Your beautiful names, by Your strength, Your power, and Your might, by all that Your knowledge encompasses, by the right of Your Messenger, and by Your Greatest Name, the most Great, by which whoever calls upon You, it is binding upon You to answer him and not to turn him back, that You grant him his request, that You forgive me all my sins and all that You know of me."

Then ask Allah for your needs of this world and the next, and say often: "O Allah, I ask You for the deliverance of my neck from the Fire." Repeat this supplication and others, whatever is made easy for you and opened to you, and multiply your exaltation, your declaration of His oneness, and your seeking of forgiveness, until the sun sets. When it sets, say: "O Allah, do not make this the last of my covenants with this standing-place, and open it to me forever as long as You keep me alive. Turn me back from it triumphant and successful, my supplication answered, my sin forgiven, returning with the best return of any of Your delegation who came to You and any of the pilgrims of Your Sacred House. Make me today among the most honored of Your delegation before You, and grant me the best of what You have granted any of them of good and blessing, of mercy and pleasure and well-being. Bless me in that to which I return, of family and wealth, of little and much, and bless them in me. O Allah, expand for us Your lawful and wholesome provision, and do not plot against me nor draw me on by degrees, and turn away from me the evil of the corrupt among jinn and mankind, and send blessings upon Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, as You are worthy and as they are worthy of You."

He said: whoever falls ill and loses consciousness is to be stood at the standing-place of Arafah. And he said: the standing at Arafah is not valid except in a state of purity. It is narrated from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and his family) that he said: "The gravest in sin of the people of Arafah is he who departs from it believing that he has not been forgiven." Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali explained: he means the one who despairs of Allah's mercy. And Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali said one day, having stood at Arafah: "Allah is a Lord of generosity. By Allah, not one who pours forth from this standing-place this evening, neither the righteous nor the corrupt, departs without a share from Allah. As for the righteous, he is answered for his Hereafter and his world; and as for the corrupt, he is given his portion and his lot of what he asked for of this world." And al-Husayn ibn Ali saw a man begging from the people on the day of Arafah, and said, rebuking him: "Woe to you. Do you ask of any but Allah on this day, when even those in the wombs are hoped for on this day?" And Abu Jafar ibn Muhammad would not turn away any beggar on the day of Arafah. And Jafar ibn Muhammad would increase greatly on that day in supplication, prayer, charity, and the seeking of forgiveness.

### The departure from Arafah to Muzdalifah

Allah Almighty said: "Then pour forth from where the people pour forth." Abu Abdullah Jafar ibn Muhammad said: the Quraysh used to pour forth from Muzdalifah in the age of ignorance, saying, "We are more entitled to the House than the rest of the people." So Allah Almighty commanded them to pour forth from Arafat, from where the people pour forth; for before that they had not been standing at Arafat, but only at the Sacred Precinct, and they were commanded to stand at Arafat and to pour forth from it.

One does not depart from Arafah until the sun has set and its disc has disappeared. When that has happened, the people pour forth toward Muzdalifah, and they do not pray Maghrib until they have reached it, combining there the Maghrib and the later Isha prayer with the imam, with a single adhan and two iqamahs. They pass the night at Muzdalifah, the night of the Day of Sacrifice, and the best place is that which is near the Sacred Precinct. Whoever pours forth from Arafah before the sun sets owes a camel, which he slaughters, or else he gives its price in charity. And when he pours forth he should increase in the remembrance of Allah Almighty and in seeking forgiveness, for Allah Almighty says: ﴿ثُمَّ أَفِيضُوا مِنْ حَيْثُ أَفَاضَ النَّاسُ وَاسْتَغْفِرُوا اللَّهَ إِنَّ اللَّهَ غَفُورٌ رَحِيمٌ﴾ "Then pour forth from where the people pour forth, and seek Allah's forgiveness; indeed Allah is Forgiving, Merciful." [al-Baqarah: 199].

He should be measured in his going. When the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and his family) departed from Arafah in the Farewell Pilgrimage, riding upon his she-camel al-Qaswa, he drew in her rein until her head nearly touched his foot, and he was saying, gesturing with his right hand, "O people, tranquility, tranquility." And whenever he came upon a hill among the hills he would loosen her rein a little that she might climb, until he reached Muzdalifah, where he combined the two evening prayers.

Abu Abdullah Jafar ibn Muhammad was asked about praying the two evening prayers short of Muzdalifah, and he said, "No," even if a third of the night should pass; and whoever does so deliberately owes a sacrifice. And he said: to pass the night at Muzdalifah on the night of the Day of Sacrifice is of the Sunnah. Whoever deliberately does not pass the night there, knowing it to be of the Sunnah, owes a camel, unless he returns within what remains of the night and stands there. But whoever left it out of ignorance, and it has already passed, having stood at Muzdalifah even for a moment, owes nothing. It is recommended for the imam to stand in Muzdalifah at al-Quzah, which is the mountain upon which the standing is made; and all of Muzdalifah is a valid standing-place. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and his family) said: "All of Arafah is a standing-place, and all of Mina is a place of sacrifice." He also granted a dispensation to the women and the weak to pour forth from Muzdalifah by night, to cast the jamarat by night, and to pray Fajr at Mina; but the imam and the rest of the people do not depart from Muzdalifah until they have prayed Fajr and the daylight has fully broken.

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and his family) stood at Muzdalifah until the sun shone upon Thabir, which is the first mountain in the world upon which the sun rises. When he saw it he said, "Shine, O Thabir; shine, O Thabir. I say as my brother Musa said: My Lord, expand for me my breast, and make my affair easy for me, and untie the knot from my tongue, that they may understand my speech; and appoint for me a helper from my family, Ali my brother; strengthen my back through him, and let him share in my affair."

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: when you stand at the Sacred Precinct, praise Allah and extol Him, and recite of the verses of the Quran what you are able, then pray upon the Prophet and his family, then supplicate for yourself as much as you are able. For the Messenger of Allah (may Allah bless him and his family), when he had prayed Fajr on the Day of Sacrifice at Muzdalifah, mounted al-Qaswa until he came to the Sacred Precinct, where he ascended and faced the qibla, and exalted Allah, declared His oneness, and proclaimed Him alone, and did not cease standing thus until the daylight had fully broken. Then he poured forth from Muzdalifah toward Mina until he came to Muhassir, the valley between Muzdalifah and Mina, where he quickened his pace a little and took the middle road that brings one out at the great jamrah.

From Muzdalifah the Messenger of Allah (ص) came down toward Mina, and when he reached Muhassir, the valley that lies between Muzdalifah and Mina, he quickened his pace a little and took the middle road that brings the pilgrim out at the Great Jamrah. He went on until he reached the jamrah beside the tree and pelted it with seven pebbles. Then he came to Mina, offered his sacrifice, and shaved his head. Hastening through Muhassir is a Sunnah, and one should hasten through it as the Messenger of Allah (ص) did. On that day the Jamrat al-Aqabah is thrown before the sacrifice; then one slaughters and shaves, and there is no harm in whichever of these he begins with.

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: the pebbles for the jamarat may be taken from Muzdalifah or from other places lying toward Mina. Whoever is at Mina should not carry pebbles from one jamrah to another; rather, the pebbles for all the jamarat are taken from a single place. It is recommended that they be gathered up one by one, dark and speckled, about the size of a fingertip, and it is disliked to break them off from larger stones. It is recommended to perform the ritual washing before throwing them. The one who throws should keep Mina on his right and the House on his left when he means to throw, and should throw from the floor of the valley, not from above it. Abu Jafar ibn Ali said: when you throw the jamarat, pronounce the takbir with each pebble you cast.

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: whoever throws the Jamrat al-Aqabah on the Day of Sacrifice before slaughtering, then comes to Mina and slaughters and shaves, then proceeds to the House and circumambulates it seven times and performs the say between al-Safa and al-Marwah seven times as well, and this is the circumambulation of the Hajj, then returns and performs the circumambulation of the women, when he has done all of this, everything becomes lawful to him that ihram had forbidden to the muhrim by reason of his consecration. He then returns to Mina and remains there through the days of Tashriq, throwing the jamarat each day after the sun has passed its zenith. Each jamrah is pelted with seven pebbles, beginning with the lesser, then the middle, then the greater. He has latitude to throw at any hour of the day, though it is preferable that he be in a state of purity. The Messenger of Allah (ص) used to walk to the jamarat when he threw them.

Ali threw the jamarat both on foot and mounted, and there is no fixed prescription in this. The Messenger of Allah (ص) granted the herdsmen a dispensation to throw the jamarat by night, and whoever misses throwing them by day may throw them by night. Whoever places the pebbles of one jamrah upon another should repeat the throwing, beginning with the lesser jamrah and ending with the greater, which is the Jamrat al-Aqabah. The jamarat are thrown on behalf of the sick, and whoever misses any part of the throwing makes it up when he remembers, or appoints someone to make it up for him. Whoever hastens his departure within two days leaves the pebbles that remain with him and does not throw them at the jamarat after he has departed.

> وَلَا تَحْلِقُوا۟ رُءُوسَكُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَبْلُغَ ٱلْهَدْىُ مَحِلَّهُۥ

As for the sacrificial offering, Allah Almighty said: "They are the ones who disbelieved and turned you away from the Sacred Mosque, and the sacrificial offerings from reaching their place of slaughter" [al-Fath: 25]. And He, Blessed and Exalted, said: "Do not shave your heads until the sacrificial offering reaches its place of slaughter" [al-Baqarah: 196]. The place of slaughter for the offering brought in a single Hajj, or in a Hajj joined to an Umrah, is Mina; the place of slaughter for the offering brought in a single Umrah is Mecca; and each is slaughtered in its own place. When the Messenger of Allah (ص) brought forward his offering he slaughtered it at the place of slaughter in Mina and said: "This is the place of sacrifice, and all of Mina is a place of sacrifice." So the people slaughtered at their own encampments. It is preferable that a man attend to the slaughter of his offering and his sacrificial animal himself, or slaughter it with his own hand; and if he is unable, let his hand be together with the hand of the one who slaughters; and if he is unable even for that, let him stand over it while the butcher acts in his stead. He should entrust this only to a Muslim.

The Messenger of Allah (ص) made Ali a partner in his offering. All the offerings that the Messenger of Allah (ص) brought, and those that Ali brought from Yemen, amounted to one hundred camels. The Messenger of Allah (ص) slaughtered sixty-three of them with his own hand, bound and standing, drawing near to him one after another, and he commanded Ali, who slaughtered the rest. Then he ordered that a portion from each be placed in a pot and cooked; and the Messenger of Allah (ص) and Ali ate of that flesh and sipped of its broth, so that they might partake of every one of them, for Allah has said: "when their flanks fall, eat of them."

When Ali slaughtered his offering or sacrificed his devoted animal, he would say: "I have turned my face toward the One who created the heavens and the earth, as a sincere Muslim, and I am not among the polytheists. Truly my prayer, my devotion, my life, and my death are for Allah, the Lord of the worlds; no partner has He. Thus have I been commanded, and I am among the Muslims. O Allah, this is from You and to You. In the name of Allah." Then he took the knife to it. The naming of Allah suffices for that, and its omission is not permitted except for one who forgets; when he remembers, he names Allah. Whoever cannot find an offering to bring on his own should share with others in whatever he is able and can manage, and the more he does of this, the better.

The best of offerings is the she-camel, then the male camel, then the female of the cattle, for it was narrated from Ali that the female of the cattle is preferable to the male; and the name "badanah" applies to both camels and cattle. Then come the males of the cattle, then the ram of the sheep, which is better than the muja, which is the one whose testicles have been bound or crushed; and the muja is better than the mustalim, which is the one whose testicles have been cut away; and this is better than the ewe; and the ewe is better than the ram of the goats; and the ram of the goats is better than the female of the goats. None of all these suffices except the mature animal or the thani, save the sheep, of which the yearling suffices. Best of these is the mulh, whose whiteness is mingled with black in places, so that its muzzle is black, as are the sockets about its eyes, the extremities of its fore and hind legs, and the tip of its tail; and it was said that this is the description of the ram that was sent down to Ibrahim.

Among offerings and sacrificial animals the plump is preferred, and the lean is disliked unless a man did not intend it, having bought it fat only for it to grow thin in his keeping. They forbade the animal whose horn is wholly broken; and if only the outer part is broken, it is called aqsam. They directed that the eye and the ear be inspected carefully, meaning that one look to this so that there be no defect. They forbade the animal whose udder teats, that is, the nipples of the udder, have been severed; and likewise the mangy, the aged, and the one whose ear has been cut off; and they disapproved of the muqabalah, whose ear has been cut at the front, and the mudabarah, whose ear has been cut at the back, and the sharqa, whose ear has been split in two, and the kharqa, in whose ear there is a round hole. As for a defect that befalls the animal after a man has bought it and dedicated it as an offering, nothing is required of him, and it suffices him. Its dedication is effected by marking it or garlanding it.

The marking of camels and cattle is that they be pierced in the hump on the right side with a piece of iron until their blood flows over it, so that the animal may be known as an offering should it stray. The ox is garlanded, and the sheep too when it is offered. The camel is draped with a covering cloth, which is given in charity when it is slaughtered. If the camel is disabled before it reaches its destination, it is slaughtered, a sandal is dipped in its blood and it is garlanded with it, and it is left so that whoever passes by of those for whom eating the offering is lawful may know that it is an offering and cut from it and eat, if he wishes. Of these offerings, what is voluntary its owner may eat from and feed to whomever he wishes; but what is obligatory, which is that due for a vow, or as recompense for game hunted, or for spoiling some part of the Hajj, of that he eats nothing. And the offering may not be sold, whether it is obligatory or otherwise.

Whoever's offering is disabled before it reaches its destination and who then finds no replacement bears nothing further; and if it strays, he buys another in a different place. If the offering he buys is the obligatory one, that is, if he has marked or garlanded it, he slaughters both together; if he has not dedicated it, then he has a choice in the matter. If a man's offering is misled together with the one who bought it, its owner may take it if he wishes, and it suffices for him but does not suffice for the one who slaughtered it; and he may claim its price, if he wishes, from the one who sold it to him. Whoever finds a stray offering should announce it, and if he finds no one who seeks it, he slaughters it on the last of the days of Tashriq on behalf of its owner. Whoever has his offering slaughtered and then has part of it stolen, it suffices for him. They made obligatory the presentation of the offering, which is that a man take it from Mina to Arafah and stand with it there, and at Muzdalifah, and at all the ritual stations, and then slaughter it. Whoever slaughters a voluntary offering may eat of it, give charity from it, and feed of it whatever he wishes; and there is no fixed time for this.

Abu Abd Allah Jafar ibn Muhammad was asked about the words of Allah Almighty, "So eat of it and feed the poor and the wretched," and His saying, "eat of it and feed the contented and the beggar who importunes." He said: the poor man is the one who does not beg of people, and the wretched is in a harsher state than he and more sorely pressed. The contented is the one who is satisfied with what is given to him, who does not grow angry, nor cry out, nor twist his mouth in vexation. The beggar who importunes is the one who passes by presenting himself. Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali used to test the people in this so that the contented might be known from others: when a beggar came to a stop before him, he ordered that the head of the sacrificial animal be given to him. If he took it and passed on rejoicing, not vexed, he ordered that he be given of the meat as well; but if he was vexed and left it and went his way, he knew that he was not of the contented.

They disliked that anything of the offerings be taken out of Mina, except a small thing to make use of, such as some of the hump for medicine, or something of the hide, the wool, the fur, the sinew, or the hair, for such as one has need of on a journey. They favored that the hides of the offerings and sacrificial animals be given in charity, though they permitted their use, and that the butcher be given some of them and of the covering cloth. They forbade the offering of the jallalah, which is the animal that feeds upon dung and filth, and the eating of its flesh, until it has been cleansed by fodder and confined for a known period; and likewise its milk is not to be drawn until it has been cleared: the she-camel and the camel forty days, cattle twenty days, sheep seven days, ducks five days, and chickens three days. They forbade riding the offering in a way that would harm or weaken it, and loading burdens upon it, and putting it to work in the same manner; but there is no harm in what neither weakens it nor injures it. Whoever forgets to slaughter at Mina slaughters at Mecca when he remembers. The days of Mina are three following the Day of Sacrifice. The day after the Day of Sacrifice is the first of them, and it is called the Day of Qarr, because one settles at Mina upon it and does not depart, nor leave Mina upon it except to visit the House and circumambulate. The second is the Day of the First Departure, as Allah Almighty said: "Whoever hastens within two days, there is no sin upon him," meaning the hastening of the departure from Mina. The third is the last of the two days of departure.

As for the shaving and the shortening, Allah Almighty said: "Allah has fulfilled His Messenger's vision in truth: you shall surely enter the Sacred Mosque, if Allah wills, in safety, with heads shaved and hair shortened" [al-Fath: 27]. Shaving is what is done with the razor, and shortening is what is taken with scissors from the ends of the hair, leaving some of it. It has been narrated from the Ahl al-Bayt that shaving is better than shortening, and that the Messenger of Allah (ص) shaved in the Farewell Pilgrimage, and some of the people shaved and some of them shortened. The Messenger of Allah (ص) said, "O Allah, have mercy upon those who shave." One who heard him said, "And those who shorten, O Messenger of Allah?" He said, "O Allah, have mercy upon those who shave and those who shorten." They ordered that with the shaving one take also from the sides of the beard and the mustache and pare the nails, and that the razor reach to the two prominent bones beneath the temples level with the ears. We have mentioned that the bald man and the one whose head is close-shorn each passes the razor over his head. The woman, when she comes out of ihram, cuts something from the ends of her hair and does not shave her head or any part of it.

Whoever forgets to shave, or does not know of it, until he has departed from Mina should shave wherever he recalls or learns of it; and if he is near to Mina he returns to it and shaves there; and if he shaves after he has drawn far from it, he sends his hair to Mina and has it cast there, if he is able. They ordered that whatever falls of the hair be buried. It has been narrated from Ali that he said, "Bury this hair, for everything that falls from the son of Adam while he is alive is dead matter that must be buried." Ali ibn al-Husayn used to bury his hair in a hollow at Mina when he shaved, saying at that time, "O Allah, grant me for every hair a light on the Day of Resurrection."

> ثُمَّ لْيَقْضُوا۟ تَفَثَهُمْ وَلْيُوفُوا۟ نُذُورَهُمْ وَلْيَطَّوَّفُوا۟ بِٱلْبَيْتِ ٱلْعَتِيقِ

As for what the pilgrim does at Mina, Allah Almighty said: "Then let them complete their rites and fulfill their vows and circumambulate the Ancient House" [al-Hajj: 29]. It has been narrated from Ali that he said: the completing of the rites is the throwing, the shaving, the vows, the walking, and the sacrifices; and the circumambulation of the Ancient House is the Circumambulation of the Visit, which is the Circumambulation of the Overflowing, the Circumambulation of the Hajj, of which I have already spoken in the chapter on Circumambulation. It falls on the Day of Sacrifice: the people visit the House after they have shaved and slaughtered at Mina, then return to the House and circumambulate it, then return to Mina, having become free of everything from which their consecration had bound them, as I have mentioned; and it matters not with which he begins, whether with shaving or with slaughtering. It is recommended thereafter to hasten the Circumambulation of the Overflowing on the Day of Sacrifice for the one performing tamattu, while they permitted the qarin and the one performing Hajj alone to delay it. There is no harm in throwing the jamarat before shaving and slaughtering, or after them; but one does not make the visit before he has shaved, slaughtered, and thrown, unless he does so forgetfully or in ignorance, in which case nothing is required of him, and he shaves, slaughters, and throws when he turns back. And he does not spend the nights of Mina away from Mina; whoever spends a night away from it deliberately owes a blood offering for each night he spends away, and whoever does so in ignorance or forgetfulness owes nothing, and Allah forgives them.

It is related that the Messenger of Allah (ص) shortened his prayer in Mina and commanded that Allah the Almighty be remembered abundantly during the days spent there. Jafar ibn Muhammad commented on the words of Allah the Almighty, ﴿فَإِذَا قَضَيْتُم مَّنَاسِكَكُمْ فَاذْكُرُوا اللَّهَ كَذِكْرِكُمْ آبَاءَكُمْ أَوْ أَشَدَّ ذِكْرًا﴾ "When you have fulfilled your rituals, remember Allah as you remember your fathers, or with even greater remembrance" [Al-Baqarah: 200]. He said that when the polytheists would settle in Mina, they would boast of their fathers and recount the glories of their ancestors, and so Allah the Almighty commanded the Muslims to put in the place of that the remembrance of the Almighty.

They held it disapproved to fast on the day of the sacrifice and the days of Tashreeq. They said that when the pilgrim throws the Jamarat al-Aqaba al-Kubra on the day of sacrifice, he is released from everything that was forbidden to him in the state of ihram, except hunting, women, dwelling outside of Mina, and perfume. When he then performs the circumambulation of the House and the say between al-Safa and al-Marwah, everything that was forbidden to him in ihram becomes permissible, except women; and once he performs the Tawaf al-Nisa, everything becomes permissible to him. They said that the takbir during the days of Mina is to be said after every obligatory prayer, the worshipper saying it after the taslim and before he rises: "Allahu Akbar, Allahu Akbar, la ilaha ill-Allah, wa Allahu Akbar, Allahu Akbar wa lillahi al-hamd. Allahu Akbar for what He has guided us to, and Allahu Akbar for what He has provided us of the grazing livestock." He may visit the House each day of Mina if he wishes and perform voluntary circumambulation as much as he wishes during the day, then return and spend the night in Mina.

Concerning the departure from Mina, Allah the Almighty said, ﴿وَاذْكُرُواْ اللَّهَ فِى أَيَّامٍ مَعْدُودَاتٍ فَمَن تَعَجَّلَ فِى يَوْمَيْنِ فَلاَ إِثْمَ عَلَيْهِ وَمَن تَأَخَّرَ فَلا إِثْمَ عَلَيْهِ لِمَنِ اتَّقَى﴾ "And remember Allah during the appointed days. Whoever hastens in two days, there is no sin upon him, and whoever delays, there is no sin upon him, for the one who fears Allah" [Al-Baqarah: 203]. Abu Abd Allah Jafar ibn Muhammad said that this refers to the stay in Mina: if you wish to remain, you have done well, and if you wish to hasten your departure in two days, you may do so, as Allah the Almighty has said. Whoever hastens his departure on the second day of Tashreeq, which is the third day after the day of sacrifice, should not depart until he has prayed the noon prayer and thrown the jamarat; thereafter he may depart if he wishes, between then and sunset. If the sun sets upon him, he stays the night, and then belongs to the people of the third day and may depart whenever he wishes, from the beginning of the day to its end, after he has prayed Fajr and thrown the jamarat. It is mentioned that one should send his baggage ahead toward Makkah before the departure. It is recommended for whoever departs from Mina to descend at al-Muhassab, which is al-Bat-ha, and to tarry there a little before continuing on. So the Messenger of Allah (ص) did: when he came to al-Muhassab he descended, remained for an hour, and then set out again.

It was said that whoever is among the first to depart may remain in Makkah until the people have shaved. When the people move on to Makkah, whoever desires to enter the House should enter it if he is able, and whoever does not enter owes nothing. Abu Abd Allah Jafar ibn Muhammad said: If you are able to enter the House, enter it whenever you wish; but if you fear the crowd, do not endanger yourself. And he mentioned the account of a people who perished in the crush within it.

He said that it is recommended for whoever wishes to enter the Kaaba to perform a ritual bath. Upon entering, after praising Allah as He deserves and sending prayers upon the Prophet and his family, one says: "O Allah, You have said, and whoever enters it is safe, so make me safe from Your punishment, and cleanse my heart of arrogance, hypocrisy, ostentation, and doubt regarding Your religion, and secure me from my fear of the Fire on the Day of Resurrection. O Allah, whoever has arrayed himself, or equipped himself, or made ready, or prepared for a journey to some created being in hope of his gift and his bounty, to You, my Master, has been my arraying, my equipping, and my preparation, in hope of Your gift and Your bounty. So do not disappoint my hope today, and do not turn me away today in disappointment from Your House. You have made it sacred, and You are the most generous of those whose house is visited. O Most Great, there is no god but You."

He said that whoever wishes to pray voluntarily inside the House should do so, and it is recommended to pray between the two pillars, upon the red marble, for there is no spot on earth better than it, and upon it the Messenger of Allah (ص) prayed. He then rose and faced the back of the House in his prayer, then stood and faced each corner of the House, supplicating at it, before returning to the red marble to pray upon it. He said that one does not pray an obligatory prayer inside the Kaaba, nor is it permissible to pray upon its roof.

He said that whoever intends to depart from Makkah should make his final act with it a circumambulation of the House, performing seven rounds, then bidding it farewell and placing his hand between the Black Stone and the Door, saying: "O Allah, I am Your servant, and the son of Your servant, and the son of Your handmaid. You carried me upon Your mount and conveyed me through Your land until You brought me into Your sanctuary. O Allah, this is Your House, and I have come to it, and I have hoped, in the goodness of my thought of You, that You would forgive me my sins. So if You have forgiven me, then increase Your pleasure toward me and grant me nearness with You; and if You have not yet forgiven me, then from this hour forgive me, before I depart from Your House. This is the hour of my departure, if You have granted me leave, without turning away from You, nor from Your House, nor exchanging You or Your House for any other. So guard me on my right and on my left, behind me and before me, until You bring me to my family; and when You have brought me to them, do not forsake me, and take me unto Yourself upon that upon which You have taken Your righteous servants, for You are more worthy of that than Your creation and than I." Then he clings to the House and says: "O Allah, do not make this the last of my covenant with Your House." When he has gone out, he stands at the door of the mosque, turns his face toward the House, fills his eyes with the sight of it, raises his hands toward the sky, and says: "O Allah, send me back triumphant, prospering, shown mercy, and forgiven. O Allah, if You cause me to die, forgive me; and if You grant me life, provide me a Hajj in the coming year, and do not make this the last of my covenant with Your Sacred House."

He said that it is recommended for one who intends to leave Makkah to buy dates for dirhams, or whatever he is able, and to give them in charity, intending thereby to atone for whatever befell him during his ihram and his Hajj of neglect or shortcoming. And he said that one departing from Hajj should not hurry his travel as though he were turning away from what he dislikes toward what he loves; rather, let him depart with tranquility upon him. And Ali would not accept the testimony of one who races ahead of the pilgrims. One day he went out to al-Rahbah and saw a gathering. He asked what this was, and was told it was one who had raced ahead of the pilgrims. He said: "No welcome and no greeting. You have shortened the prayer and wearied the mount at noon."

As for the individual Umrah, Allah the Almighty said, ﴿وَأَتِمُّوا الْحَجَّ وَالْعُمْرَةَ لِلّهِ﴾ "And complete Hajj and Umrah for Allah" [Al-Baqarah: 196]. The Ahl al-Bayt, and those of the reciters who agreed with them, read Umrah in the accusative, giving it the meaning of an obligation: complete the Hajj and the Umrah. They said that it is obligatory. Some of those who differed from them read "and Umrah for Allah" in the nominative, as though they cut off the speech after His words "and complete the Hajj," and began anew, so that the obligation fell upon what was for Allah the Almighty. Jafar ibn Muhammad said that Umrah is an obligation in the same rank as Hajj, because Allah the Almighty says, "Complete the Hajj and the Umrah." It was narrated from Ali that he said Umrah is obligatory. And it was narrated from al-Sadiq Jafar ibn Muhammad that he said: Umrah to Umrah is an expiation for what lies between them. The Messenger of Allah (ص) said: An Umrah in the month of Ramadan equals a Hajj. For the individual Umrah, one who performs it alone enters ihram from the miqat, avoids what the one in ihram avoids, and forms the intention. When he comes to the House he circumambulates it seven times, performs the say between al-Safa and al-Marwah, prays the two rakahs of the circumambulation, and is released; this is the Umrah. If he has driven a sacrificial animal, he slaughters it at Makkah. Al-Sadiq Jafar ibn Muhammad said: Perform Umrah in whichever of the months you wish, and the most excellent of that is an Umrah in the month of Rajab. If you perform Umrah in the month of Hajj and then depart, it is an individual Umrah; but if you then perform Hajj in that same year, you are one performing Qiran.

> وَلَا تَحْلِقُوا۟ رُءُوسَكُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَبْلُغَ ٱلْهَدْىُ مَحِلَّهُۥ

Concerning repulsion and siege, Allah the Almighty said, ﴿هُمُ الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا وَصَدُّوكُمْ عَنِ الْمَسْجِدِ الْحَرَامِ وَالْهَدْىَ مَعْكُوفًا أَن يَبْلُغَ مَحِلَّهُ﴾ "It is they who disbelieved and barred you from the Sacred Mosque, and the offering, prevented from reaching its place" [Al-Fath: 25]. And He, Blessed and Exalted, said, ﴿فَإِنْ أُحْصِرْتُمْ فَمَا اسْتَيْسَرَ مِنَ الْهَدْيِ﴾ "If you are prevented, then whatever offering is feasible" [Al-Baqarah: 196]. Al-Sadiq Jafar ibn Muhammad said that repulsion is prevention, and siege is illness. He said that the Messenger of Allah (ص) set out from Medina in the year of al-Hudaybiyyah with more than a thousand of his companions, intending Umrah. He entered ihram from Dhu al-Hulayfa, and they entered ihram, and he drove the sacrificial animals, collared them, and marked them. Word of this reached the Quraysh, and this was before the conquest of Makkah, and they gathered their forces against him and went out to bar his way. When he was near Asfan, their news came to him. He said: We have not come to fight anyone; we have come only as pilgrims of Umrah. If the Quraysh wish, I will make a truce with them for a term and leave the way open between me and the people; then, if they wish to enter into that which the people have entered, they may enter, and if they refuse, I will fight them until Allah judges between us, and He is the best of judges. The emissaries went back and forth between him and the Quraysh, and there was in that a long account, the end of which was that he (ص) made a truce with them for a term, on the terms that he would turn back that year and perform Umrah, if he wished, the following year, along with whoever wished to accompany him. He then slaughtered the sacrificial animals he had driven where he stood, and they cut their hair short, and he turned back, and the Muslims with him turned back.

This is the ruling of one who is turned back from the House, and what he must do when he has made the Hajj or the Umrah, or both together, binding upon himself, which is only when he has passed the miqat. As for one turned back before reaching the miqat, he simply turns away, whether he entered ihram or not; and if he had driven a sacrificial animal, he turns back if he wishes and does with it as he pleases, because he had not yet made it binding, since, as mentioned before, the offering is made binding by its marking and its collaring, and that is only after entering ihram from the miqat. Whoever enters ihram from short of the miqat is not in a valid ihram and has done wrong and departed from the Sunnah.

> وَلَا تَحْلِقُوا۟ رُءُوسَكُمْ حَتَّىٰ يَبْلُغَ ٱلْهَدْىُ مَحِلَّهُۥ

As for siege, it is illness, as al-Sadiq Jafar ibn Muhammad explained in the words of Allah the Almighty. He was asked about a man beset by illness who had with him an offering he had sent ahead with his companions, appointing them a set time: if he is in Hajj, the place of slaughtering the offering is on the Day of Sacrifice; and if he is in Umrah, let him reckon the measure of his companions arrival in Makkah and the hour he has appointed for them, and when that hour comes, he cuts his hair short and is released. This, again, is only when he had entered ihram from the miqat and then fell ill afterward. But if he had not yet reached the miqat, he may turn back if he wishes or remain, because the obligation of Hajj had not yet become binding upon him. It was narrated that al-Husayn ibn Ali set out from Medina intending Umrah, and when he came to al-Suqya he fell ill, and news of him reached Ali, who was in Medina. He came to him and said: My son, what do you complain of? He said: I complain of my head. So Ali called for a camel and slaughtered it on his behalf, shaved his head, and returned him to Medina; and when he recovered, he went out and performed Umrah. Likewise, whoever is besieged by illness after entering ihram for Hajj, or intending Umrah, or both together, is obliged to shed blood, as Allah the Almighty said, ﴿فَإِنْ أُحْصِرْتُمْ فَمَا اسْتَيْسَرَ مِنَ الْهَدْيِ﴾ "If you are prevented, then whatever offering is feasible" [Al-Baqarah: 196], and that is a sheep or more; then he turns back. When he recovers from his ailment, he goes out and makes up what he was besieged from of Hajj, or Umrah, or both together.

The one besieged by illness does not approach women until he completes what he was besieged from, while the one merely turned back may approach women and is not obliged to make up what he was turned back from. This is entirely the word of the Ahl al-Bayt. Al-Sadiq Jafar ibn Muhammad was asked about one who was besieged and then recovered, whether women became lawful for him. He said: No, not until he circumambulates the House. He was asked: Then why did women become lawful for the Messenger of Allah (ص) when he returned from al-Hudaybiyyah, though he had not circumambulated the House? He said: The two are not alike. For the one turned back, that is lawful; but for the one besieged, it is not.

Concerning Hajj on behalf of the incapacitated and the deceased, a woman from Khatham came to the Messenger of Allah (ص) and said: O Messenger of Allah, my father is an old man, grown too feeble to move, and the obligation of Hajj has come upon him. Shall I perform Hajj on his behalf? The Messenger of Allah (ص) said: Yes, do so, for were there a debt upon your father which you discharged, would that not suffice for him? Jafar ibn Muhammad was asked about the like of this and permitted it, citing the report of the Khathami woman. So the aged man and the aged woman who have despaired of being able to perform Hajj may each equip someone to perform Hajj on their behalf, and it suffices. But one who is able to make the journey is not discharged by another; he must go out and perform Hajj himself, whether he is able in ease or not, and even if he fears an enemy, because he is in the state of one who may hope for that fear to pass and for the way to Hajj to open, and there is no fixed time appointed for Hajj.

Hajj is an obligation once in a lifetime. As long as a person still hopes to be able to perform it, the obligation remains upon him; and if he finds a way to it at last and yet delays, he has wronged himself.

Hajj may be performed on behalf of one who has died, whether the deceased bequeathed it or his guardian volunteers to equip someone to perform it for him. If the deceased bequeathed that Hajj be performed on his behalf from the third of his estate, it is taken from that third. If he did not specify the third and had not yet performed his obligatory Hajj, it is taken from the whole of his estate, and any further bequests he made after that are paid from the third.

Whoever is engaged to perform Hajj on behalf of another, and finds that the wage exceeds his expenses, may keep the surplus, and the Hajj is credited both to him and to the one on whose behalf he performs it. One who has not yet performed Hajj for himself is only a hired man in this; if he has already performed his own Hajj, that is better. A man may perform Hajj on behalf of a man, and a woman on behalf of a woman. If a woman performs Hajj on behalf of a man, it suffices him when he can find no other, or when she is more capable than the men available to him and more exact in performing the rites. Whoever performs Hajj on behalf of another should strive to complete the Hajj in full and to discharge all its rites.

It was narrated of Jafar ibn Muhammad that, after his son Ismail (ع) died, he hired a man to perform Hajj on his behalf, instructed him in what to do during the Hajj, and stipulated its conditions upon him. Then he said to him: "Know that if you fulfill what we have stipulated of you, it will count as a Hajj for the one on whose behalf you have performed it, and you will have a reward for keeping the condition laid upon you and for the labor of your body." And the Messenger of Allah (ص) said that Allah, mighty and majestic, admits three into Paradise on account of a single Hajj: the one who bequeaths that Hajj be performed for him, the one who carries out his bequest after him, and the pilgrim who performs it on his behalf, if he strives and fulfills the duty upon him.

Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali said that there is no harm if one who performs Hajj for another for a wage should add, on his own behalf as a volunteer, whatever circumambulation or Umrah he wishes, once he has discharged what he was engaged to do. He also said: whoever performs Hajj on behalf of another, let him say from the moment of his ihram, "اللَّهُمَّ إِنِّي أُرِيدُ الْحَجَّ عَنْ فُلَانٍ فَتَقَبَّلْ مِنْهُ وَأْجِرْنِي عَلَى قَضَائِي عَنْهُ" — O God, I intend the Hajj on behalf of so-and-so; accept it from him and reward me for discharging it on his behalf. Let him hold this intention and pronounce it at every station, at every sacred site and landmark, and in every act among the acts of Hajj, whether obligatory or recommended.

**Missed Hajj and Umrah**

Whoever performs the Hajj of tamattu, joining Umrah to Hajj, or the Hajj of qiran, and does not reach Mecca until a time when he fears that performing the circumambulation and the running of his Umrah would cause him to miss the standing, let him hasten instead to the standing so that his Hajj may be completed, make it a single Hajj, and perform the Umrah afresh afterward.

Whoever comes upon the people after they have left Mina and are standing at Arafat, and reaches the standing at Arafat before the sun sets, has attained the Hajj, even if he did not pass the night at Mina. Jafar ibn Muhammad said: likewise, if he reaches Arafat after the people have already poured forth from it, stands there through the night and remembers Allah, then comes to Jam before the people pour forth from Muzdalifah, he has attained the Hajj. But if he does not reach Muzdalifah until after the people have poured forth from it and the sun has risen, he has missed the Hajj.

One who has missed the Hajj should convert his ihram into Umrah, and the Hajj remains due from him for a future occasion, unless at the time of his ihram he stipulated that his release would be wherever he is prevented, in which case no Hajj is due from him for the missed occasion. Even then, Hajj remains upon him if he is one who has not yet performed his obligatory Hajj though he was able to find the way. Likewise, if he does not catch the people until after the days of Hajj have passed, let him circumambulate the House and make it a single Umrah, if he had been performing qiran or tamattu. But if he had entered ihram for Hajj alone, he owes no Umrah, because he neither entered ihram for Umrah nor intended it, unless he returns to the miqat and enters ihram for it there.

**The Vow of Hajj**

Allah, mighty and majestic, said: "عَيْنًا يَشْرَبُ بِهَا عِبَادُ اللَّهِ يُفَجِّرُونَهَا تَفْجِيرًا ۝ يُوفُونَ بِالنَّذْرِ وَيَخَافُونَ يَوْمًا كَانَ شَرُّهُ مُسْتَطِيرًا" — A fountain from which the servants of Allah drink, making it gush forth abundantly; they fulfill their vows and fear a Day whose evil is far-reaching [Al-Insan: 6-7]. We shall set out in the chapters on vows what of them is binding and what is not, God willing.

Whoever makes a binding vow to perform Hajj must fulfill it; and if he had not yet performed his obligatory Hajj, it suffices him in place of the Hajj of Islam. Abu Abdillah Jafar ibn Muhammad said that if he intends this and pronounces it, it is credited to him. Likewise, if he performs Hajj on behalf of another while he had also vowed to perform Hajj, that fulfills his vow and is credited to him.

It was narrated of Ali that he said: "Whoever vows to perform Hajj and is then unable to do so, let him send his sacrificial animal and enter ihram, remaining in the state of ihram among his household until it is sacrificed on his behalf." And Jafar ibn Muhammad said of one who makes a binding vow to walk to the Sacred House of Allah: let him walk; and when he grows weary and can no longer walk, let him ride and offer a sacrificial animal, for Allah does not burden a soul beyond its capacity. When he has given the full measure of his effort, nothing further is upon him. A binding vow is one made in obedience; but whoever vows to commit a sin has nothing binding upon him.

## 9. The Sword and the State: Jihad, Governance, and Justice

**The Obligation of Jihad and the Virtues Therein**

> أُذِنَ لِلَّذِينَ يُقَتَلُونَ بِأَنَّهُمْ ظُلِمُوا۟ ۚ وَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ عَلَىٰ نَصْرِهِمْ لَقَدِيرٌ

Allah Almighty said: "Fighting is ordained for you, though it is hateful to you. It may be that you dislike something that is good for you, and it may be that you love something that is bad for you." [Al-Baqarah: 216] He said: "When the sacred months have passed, slay the polytheists wherever you find them." [Al-Tawbah: 5] And He said: "Permission is granted to those who are fought against because they have been wronged, and Allah is indeed able to grant them victory; those who were driven from their homes unjustly, only because they said, Our Lord is Allah." [Al-Hajj: 39–40]

Al-Sadiq, Jafar ibn Muhammad (ع), said: The earth and all that is in it belongs to Allah, to His friends, and to the believers among His servants. Whatever of it lies in the hands of the people of disbelief and misguidance, they hold only by usurpation. From this, what the believers overcome and reclaim from the hands of the polytheists is called fay. Fay means "return"; one says faa al-fay, that is, the shadow returned. Allah Almighty said: "But if they return, then Allah is Forgiving, Merciful" [Al-Baqarah: 226], meaning that they returned to their wives. Likewise, whatever the people of truth recover of what had been in the hands of the people of falsehood is among that which Allah has restored to them, that is, returned to them. From this comes His saying, "What Allah restored to His Messenger" [Al-Hashr: 7], meaning returned to him. And this is the meaning of His words, "Permission is granted to those who are fought against because they have been wronged" [Al-Hajj: 39]: the people of truth are meant, while those who wrong them are the people of falsehood.

Because the people of falsehood had overcome what Allah Almighty had granted to the people of truth, He gave the people of truth permission to fight those who had oppressed them and seized what He had made theirs. He made it obligatory upon the Muslims to wage jihad against the disbelievers among those who were given the Book, until they either submit or pay the jizyah while in a state of subjection. He said, exalted be the Speaker: "Fight those who do not believe in Allah or the Last Day, who do not forbid what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden, and who do not follow the religion of truth from among those who were given the Book, until they pay the jizyah from their hand while they are subdued." [Al-Tawbah: 29] And He made obligatory upon them the fighting of the polytheists who are not People of the Book until they submit, saying, exalted be the Speaker: "And fight the polytheists all together as they fight you all together." [Al-Tawbah: 36] And He said: "When the sacred months have passed, slay the polytheists wherever you find them, and capture them, besiege them, and sit in wait for them at every place of ambush. But if they repent, establish prayer, and give zakah, then let them go their way." [Al-Tawbah: 5]

From the polytheists who are not People of the Book, nothing was accepted but Islam or the sword. From the People of the Book, the jizyah was accepted on the condition that they submit to the rule of Islam, until Allah Almighty guides whom He guides among them and he turns to Him, or until there perishes upon his error whoever perishes, the proof having already been established against them.

> ٱنفِرُوا۟ خِفَافًۭا وَثِقَالًۭا وَجَهِدُوا۟ بِأَمْوَلِكُمْ وَأَنفُسِكُمْ فِى سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ

Allah Almighty urged jihad in His path in His Book and upon the tongue of His Messenger (ص); He commanded it and exhorted to it. He said, exalted be His praise: "Go forth, light and heavy, and strive with your wealth and your selves in the way of Allah." [Al-Tawbah: 41] Al-Sadiq, Jafar ibn Muhammad, said concerning the words of Allah, "Go forth, light and heavy": He said, that is, the young and the old.

Allah, blessed and exalted, said: "Indeed, Allah has purchased from the believers their lives and their wealth in exchange for Paradise. They fight in the way of Allah, so they slay and are slain. It is a promise binding upon Him in the Torah, the Gospel, and the Quran. And who is more faithful to his covenant than Allah? So rejoice in the sale you have contracted with Him, and that is the great triumph." [Al-Tawbah: 111]

Abu Abdullah, Jafar ibn Muhammad, was asked about this verse. It was said to him: "Is everyone who strives in the way of Allah among the people of this verse?" He (ع) said: "No. Allah Almighty said that He purchased from the believers their lives and their wealth in exchange for Paradise, then He promised them further, and He described those who deserve this after the verse with their qualities, saying: The repentant, the worshippers, the praisers, the wanderers, the bowing, the prostrating, those who enjoin what is good and forbid what is evil, and those who observe the limits of Allah; and give glad tidings to the believers. [Al-Tawbah: 112]" So whoever possesses these qualities is among those to whom Allah Almighty has given the promise stated in the verse. But whoever does not possess these qualities and strives seeking the fleeting gains of this world and its glory, or is coerced into jihad while not keeping his religion and not being among the people of this description that Allah Almighty set out in His Book, is among those of whom the Messenger of Allah (ص) said: "Allah will surely support this religion through a people who have no share in it."

The Messenger of Allah (ص) said: "The strivers in the way of Allah are the leaders of the people of Paradise." And the Prophet (ص) said: "Concerning every comfort a man will be questioned, save what was in the way of Allah." And he (ص) said: "Above every act of righteousness is another act of righteousness, until a man is slain in the way of Allah," meaning that there is no righteousness above that. And he (ص) said: "There is no drop more beloved to Allah than a drop of blood shed in the way of Allah, or a drop of tears shed from fear of Allah." And he (ص) said that Allah and His angels send blessings upon the owners of horses, upon whoever keeps and prepares them in the way of Allah. And he (ص) said: "The provision spent on the horses tethered in the way of Allah is the very spending of which Allah Almighty said: Those who spend their wealth by night and by day, secretly and openly. [Al-Baqarah: 274]"

Ali (ع) said: "Strive in the way of Allah with your hands; if you are unable, then strive with your tongues; and if you are unable, then strive with your hearts." And he said: "Jihad in the way of Allah is a gate among the gates of Paradise." And he said concerning the words of Allah Almighty, "And the garment of taqwa, that is best" [Al-Araf: 26]: "It is the garment of weapons in the way of Allah."

He said: Jihad in the way of Allah is an obligation upon all Muslims, by the words of Allah Almighty, "Fighting is ordained for you." If a group of the Muslims undertakes the jihad and suffices in it, the obligation is lifted from the rest who remain behind, until those who have undertaken it come to need reinforcement. If they need it, then it becomes obligatory upon all the Muslims to reinforce them until they are sufficed. And he recited the words of Allah Almighty, "It was not for the believers to go forth all at once" [Al-Tawbah: 122], meaning when a group of them has undertaken the jihad.

The Messenger of Allah (ص) said: There is no obligation of jihad upon the bedouin among the Muslims except when they are needed, and they have no share in the fay so long as they do not strive. The likes of the bedouin today are the likes of the people of the outlying lands, the deserts, and the towns who do not fight well and have no desire for jihad. The Messenger of Allah (ص) granted the coward a dispensation to hold back from fighting, and said that a coward should not go on a raid, for he may be routed quickly; rather, let him consider what he had intended to equip himself with for the raid, and let him equip another with it, for he shall have the like of that man's reward, and nothing of his reward shall be diminished. So those we have mentioned, who are cowardly in fighting and do not know it, and who are in the condition of the bedouin, if they possess wealth, are obliged to spend it in the way of Allah, because Allah Almighty imposed jihad upon the Muslims with their selves and their wealth. Whoever is unable to strive with his person must strive with his wealth, as the Messenger of Allah (ص) said concerning the coward.

If a grave calamity should fall upon the Muslims, and we seek refuge in Allah from it, then it is a duty upon all people to go forth in the way of Allah. In this is the saying of Allah Almighty, "Go forth, light and heavy" [Al-Tawbah: 41], and His saying, "And fight the polytheists all together" [Al-Tawbah: 36]. At such a time the common people go forth, the free and the slave, the old and the young alike, until Allah Almighty repels from them what has befallen them. There is no jihad upon slaves in any circumstance other than this, unless their masters take them out to it, nor upon women, nor upon boys. Yet if a woman wishes to go out with her husband, or with a close kinsman within the forbidden degrees (مَحْرَم), to tend the sick and the wounded, to serve the strivers, and to help with whatever she is able and has the strength for, there is no harm in that. If she has a husband, she may not go out except with her husband's permission; and likewise the slave may not go out except with his master's permission.

**Supplication, Righteous Deeds, and the Etiquette of Travel in Jihad**

> وَلَمَّا بَرَزُوا۟ لِجَالُوتَ وَجُنُودِهِۦ قَالُوا۟ رَبَّنَآ أَفْرِغْ عَلَيْنَا صَبْرًۭا وَثَبِّتْ أَقْدَامَنَا وَٱنصُرْنَا عَلَى ٱلْقَوْمِ ٱلْكَفِرِينَ

Allah Almighty said: "And when they went forth against Goliath and his soldiers, they said: Our Lord, pour upon us patience, make firm our feet, and grant us victory over the disbelieving people." [Al-Baqarah: 250] And Allah Almighty said: "The Messenger has believed in what was revealed to him from his Lord, and so have the believers" [Al-Baqarah: 285], up to His words: "You are our Protector, so grant us victory over the disbelieving people." [Al-Baqarah: 286]

The Messenger of Allah (ص) said: No man who intends a journey appoints over his family a deputy better than two rakahs he prays at his departure, then says: "O Allah, I entrust to You myself, my family, my wealth, my religion, my worldly life, my Hereafter, my trust, and the conclusion of my deeds." No believer does this but that Allah grants him what he asked.

Jafar ibn Muhammad said: When you intend a journey, pray two rakahs, then say this saying, and then say: "O Allah, protect me and protect what is with me, and bring me and what is with me to my destination in Your fair conveyance." And ask Him your needs for what you intend of your journey, and for those of your family you leave behind, and for whatever else.

Abu Jafar, Muhammad ibn Ali (ع), said: When my father, Ali ibn al-Husayn (ع), wished to travel or to go out to some of his property, he would pray two rakahs and supplicate with whatever was easy for him when he wished to set out. Then, when he placed his foot in the stirrup, he would purchase his safety from Allah with whatever he could, meaning that he would give charity at that moment. And when Allah brought him home in safety, he would praise Allah and thank Him and again give charity with whatever was easy.

The Commander of the Believers, Ali, went out on one of his journeys, and when he was settled upon his mount he said: "Praise be to Allah, who has favored us of His bounty with what we cannot number. May Allah bless Muhammad, His Prophet, and his family. Glory be to Him who has subjected this to us, for we could not have done it ourselves, and indeed to our Lord we shall return." This is taken from the Book of Allah, from His words, "that you may settle yourselves upon it," the verse. [Az-Zukhruf: 13] And Ali said: "I bear witness that there is no god but Allah alone, having no partner, and I bear witness that Muhammad is His servant and His Messenger. O Allah, I seek refuge in You from the hardship of travel, the gloom of return, and the evil sight in family, wealth, and children. O Allah, You are the Companion in travel, the Deputy over the family, and the One whose aid is sought in every affair. Fold up for us the far distance, ease for us its ruggedness, and suffice us in what weighs upon us, for You are powerful over all things."

He used to say: It is of the way of travel that when a people set out and gather and keep company on the journey, they should pool their provisions and spend from them together, for that is more wholesome for their souls and better for the bond between them.

Al-Sadiq, Jafar ibn Muhammad, said: Manly virtue (اَلْمُرُوَّة) is of two kinds: a muruwwa in settled life and a muruwwa in travel. As for the muruwwa in settled life, it is the recitation of the Quran, the frequenting of the mosques, the company of the people of good, and the study of jurisprudence. As for the muruwwa in travel, it is generosity with one's provision, the abandonment of dispute with one's companions, and speaking well of them once they have parted.

The Messenger of Allah (ص) forbade that riding beasts be neglected until they perish, that they be burdened beyond their strength, or that a man remain seated upon one for no need. He said: Many a ridden beast is better than the one who rides it, more devoted to Allah and more given to His remembrance. And he said: A riding beast has six rights upon its owner: that he begin with its fodder when he alights, that he offer it water when he passes by water, that he not strike it except for a just cause, that he not burden it with what it cannot bear, that he not compel it to a journey beyond its strength, and that he not remain halted upon it for a period without need.

> وَلَهُۥٓ أَسْلَمَ مَن فِى ٱلسَّمَوَتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ طَوْعًۭا وَكَرْهًۭا وَإِلَيْهِ يُرْجَعُونَ

It is related of Ali that he said one day: "By Him who sent Muhammad with the truth and ennobled his household through him, there is nothing by which you are afflicted but that it is in the Quran. Whoever wishes to question me, let him question me." A man rose to him and said: "O Commander of the Believers, my riding beast has become exceedingly stubborn toward me, and I am in dread of it." He said: "Recite into its right ear: And to Him has submitted whoever is in the heavens and the earth, willingly and unwillingly, and to Him they will be returned. [Al Imran: 83]" The man did so, and his beast became docile to him.
